# Review of the Strata 4x3090 test methodology and patches (2026-10-12)

Six independent review lenses, each finding re-checked by two adversarial verifiers (facts, materiality). Status: confirmed = both verifiers confirmed; weakened = at least one verifier narrowed the claim (see corrected claim); refuted = a verifier found it wrong or already done.


## Lens: statistics

### [high] Run-to-run noise is 0.4-0.6 % sd, not +-0.3 %; one run per arm resolves ~1.2-2.5 %, so the +1 % short-prompt levers are below resolution
*key `noise-larger-and-config-dependent` · inconsistency · status: **confirmed**, reviewer confidence 0.85*

**Claim.** The +-0.3 % figure in ab_quick.sh (lines 3-4, citing TESTLOG #41) describes only the overlap-series configs. For T16-142 the three #46 repetitions at 16 users spread 1.58 % (short) and the two greedy T16-142 repeats (ab-fc vs ab-fc2) differ by -1.48 % / -1.25 %. A single run per arm can therefore resolve differences of about 1.2 % (pooled noise) to 2.5 % (T16-142-class noise) at 95 %; the predicted +0.7-1.3 % short gains (MMQ group sort, MTP batch) are not resolvable with one run, while the +3-4 % long gains are (>=5 sd).

**Evidence.** sweep.jsonl, users=16, agg_out_tps: strata-T16-142-short-r1..3 = 469.9/468.3/462.5 (range 1.58 %, sd 0.83 %); -long 342.5/343.2/340.5 (0.79 %, sd 0.41 %); T16-142-overlap-short 461.3/463.5/461.9 (sd 0.25 %), -long 345.8/347.9/346.5 (0.31 %); T16-overlap-off-short 441.1/439.4/441.9 (0.29 %), -long 327.5/326.5/327.4 (0.17 %); T16-overlap-short 437.0/435.3/438.1 (0.32 %), -long 326.8/327.2/329.0 (0.36 %). Pooled relative sd over these 8 groups (16 df) = 0.41 %; sd of a one-run A-B difference = 0.58 %, t(16)=2.12 -> 1.24 % threshold (0.71 % with 3 runs per arm). Repeated ab arms: ab-fc-T16-142-short 473.2 vs ab-fc2 466.2 (-1.48 %); -long 351.6 vs 347.2 (-1.25 %); ab-fc-T16-142fc-short 492.8 vs ab-fc2 493.1 (+0.06 %); -long 356.6 vs 357.3 (+0.20 %); ab-aux2-T16-142fca-short 492.9 vs ab-fcb-T16-142fca-short 490.5 (-0.49 %); -long 376.2 vs 377.6 (+0.37 %): rms 0.83 % -> implied single-run sd 0.59 %. The fc-family (fixed-cost switches on) is visibly tighter than T16-142, i.e. noise depends on the config. Predicted lever sizes: upstream/PROFILE-next-levers.md lines 319-323 (MMQ sort +0.7 % / +3.3 %, MTP batch +0.2 % / +1.9 %, self-commit +4.3 % / +3.3 %, QSA +4.6 % / +3.5 %). The 1-user sanity check (#57, n=2 requests) sits on a 1.3-5.8 % spread at users=1 in the same groups, so it only detects >5 % changes.

**Recommendation.** Keep one run per arm as a screen, but write the decision rule into ab_quick.sh/TESTLOG: |delta| < ~1.3 % (short) is 'not resolved'; for levers predicted at <=1.5 % run 3 pairs in alternating order (ABAB.., ~5 min GPU per extra run) or switch to the longer steady-state window (next finding), which also cuts the per-run variance. Replace the '+-0.3 %' comment with the pooled 0.4-0.6 % sd and the 1.2 % threshold (10 min of editing, 0 GPU).

- **Facts verifier: confirmed** [medium] Every number reproduces from results/sweep.jsonl (users=16): group rel. sd 0.83/0.41/0.25/0.31/0.29/0.17/0.32/0.36 %, pooled 0.413 % over 16 df, one-run-difference sd 0.584 %, x2.12 = 1.24 % (3-run means 0.71 %); repeat pairs -1.48/-1.25/+0.06/+0.20/-0.49/+0.37 %, rms 0.834 % -> 0.59 % single-run sd; users=1 ranges 1.33-5.76 %. upstream/PROFILE-next-levers.md:319-323 carries the quoted lever sizes. ab_quick.sh:4 attributes '+-0.3 %' to TESTLOG #41, but #41 contains no such figure (only ab_quick.sh:4 has it) and TESTLOG #46 already states 'Std. dev. at 16 users 1-3 tok/s' (0.2-0.9 %), so the team's own log contradicts the script comment. Two corrections lower the severity: (1) the two slow T16-142 short runs are not generic noise but a prefill-side disturbance visible in the logs: batch-admission reads 217.0 vs 204.7 ms (r3 vs r1/r2) and 213.7 vs 205.2 ms (ab-fc2 vs ab-fc), TTFT p50 1.41/1.09 vs 0.73/0.81 s, decode p50 within 1 %; without r3 the T16-142 short sd is 0.24 %, and the within-rep paired overlap-series differences are 4.1/4.1/3.8 tok/s (sd 0.17 tok/s), so undisturbed back-to-back pairs resolve well below 1 %. (2) Small levers are already screened at engine level with medians of 6 (results/levers/mmq-sort/notes.txt: -9.6 ms / -65.2 ms), not only via ab_quick. The concrete decision affected: TESTLOG #56 picked 12,24,37 over 12,25,37 on +0.4 %/+0.5 % single runs (519.2/398.0 vs 517.2/396.0; engine 523.2/405.7 vs 521.1/403.8 rows/s), below the 1.2 % threshold, i.e. an unresolved tie.
- **Materiality verifier: confirmed** [medium] Recomputed from results/sweep.jsonl (users=16): the 8 triplets give pooled rel sd 0.413 % (16 df) -> one-run A-B threshold 1.24 % (t=2.12); T16-142-short 469.9/468.3/462.5 sd 0.83 %; repeated ab arms rms 0.83 % -> implied sd 0.59 %. All as claimed. Two refinements: (a) adding the 2-rep ovq groups (16 groups, 24 df) gives pooled 0.37 %, threshold 1.07 %; (b) the four fc-family repeat pairs (fc 492.8/493.1, 356.6/357.3; fca 492.9/490.5, 376.2/377.6) have rms 0.32 % -> sd ~0.23 %, so the current default family is ~2-3x tighter than T16-142 (whose r3 outlier 462.5 coincides with TTFT p50 0.73 -> 1.41 s, an admission event). The ab_quick.sh line 3-4 '+-0.3 %' is accurate for #41 (sd 0.17-0.36 %) and TESTLOG #46 already says 'Std. dev. 1-3 tok/s'. Materiality: no adopted default rests on a sub-threshold difference on both axes (#49 +5.0/+2.2, #52 +0/+5.4, #56 +5.3/+5.8, #57 +6.5/+7.3), so no past decision flips; but the planned FP16-copies lever (+1.0/+1.2 %, PROFILE-next-levers.md 'Small but certain #6') and MMQ-sort short (+0.7 %) are below one-run resolution, and two picks were made within noise (#52, #56; see missed). Cost is lower than the lens says: an ab_quick arm takes ~2 min by file timestamps (status 02:45:42 -> long done 02:47:24 -> next status 02:47:59), so 3 pairs ~12 min GPU. Changes a decision rule and wording, not a default -> medium, not high.

### [medium] 2 rounds x 512 tokens: ramp is 11 % (short) / 20 % (long) of the wall and the last round is stall-free; short headline ~4 % below the continuous-traffic rate
*key `ramp-and-stall-free-final-round` · risk · status: **weakened**, reviewer confidence 0.8*

**Claim.** With 16 serialized admissions and all replies exactly 512 tokens, the first 3.7 s (short) / 9.0 s (long) of each arm run with partially idle slots, and the whole final round runs without admission stalls (per-slot decode 35.7 vs 33.2 tok/s short, 29.3 vs 25.6 long). The drain itself is negligible (0.12 s). The net bias differs by prompt length: the 2-round short number is 4 % below the marginal (continuous) rate, the long number is about right because the ramp penalty and the final-round bonus cancel. The ratio between arms is distorted only by a fraction of the ramp share (<=0.5 pp on a +4 % lever), so it is not decision-changing alone, but it adds to the noise problem and the absolute headline (522 tok/s short) understates continuous traffic by ~4 %.

**Evidence.** results/batchadmit/qprobe/proto.log parsed per arm (first BGEN -> 16th BADM = ramp; first BDONE after the last BADM -> last BDONE = drain): short2 wall 32.51 s, ramp 3.68 s (11.3 %), drain 0.12 s; long2 wall 44.50 s, ramp 9.01 s (20.3 %), drain 0.12 s; short4 ramp 5.0 %, long4 10.0 %. Per-slot BADM->BDONE for 512 tokens: short2 round 1 15.41 s vs round 2 14.35 s; long2 20.01 vs 17.45 s; in the 4-round runs later rounds 15.06 / 19.31 s (stalls persist while admissions continue). Marginal rate from results/batchadmit/qprobe/sweep.jsonl: short 16384/(65.28-33.33 s) = 512.8 tok/s vs 2-round 491.6 (-4.1 %); long 16384/(91.18-45.51) = 358.7 vs 360.0 (+0.4 %). bench_concurrency.py lines 102-103 return t0/t1/ttft per request but run_level (lines 106-135) keeps only percentiles, so no steady-state window can be computed from sweep.jsonl today. Engine-side cross-check: 'strata batch (pipelined...) rows/s (admissions included)' in engine-ab-fcb-T16-142fcb.log = 526.2 / 411.3 vs client 522.4 / 405.3 (0.7 % / 1.5 % overhead, identical for the fca arm: 494.0/490.5, 383.1/377.6).

**Recommendation.** Cheapest: ROUNDS=4 for lever A/Bs (ramp share halves; +32 s short / +46 s long GPU time per arm) or report the marginal R4-R2 from the same server. Proper (1-2 h scripting, same GPU time): make the run time-based (each user loops until T_end ~90 s), append a per-request JSONL (uid, t0, t_first, t1, gen, plus per-chunk timestamps = 512 floats per request), and compute throughput over [t_start+15 s, t_end-15 s]. Note in the README that the Strata-vs-vLLM rows share this protocol but vLLM admits in parallel, so the ramp costs Strata a few % more on short prompts.

- **Facts verifier: confirmed** [medium] Parsed results/batchadmit/qprobe/proto.log: short2 ramp 3.68 s (11.1 % of 33.0 s), long2 9.01 s (20.0 %), drain 0.12 s, short4/long4 ramp 5.0 %/10.0 %; per-slot BADM->BDONE short2 15.41/14.35 s, long2 20.01/17.45 s, R4 rounds 15.30/15.29/15.35/14.52 and 20.09/20.14/20.22/17.56 s (the lens's 15.06/19.31 are the round-2..4 means). qprobe/sweep.jsonl marginal rate 16384/(65.28-33.33) = 512.8 vs 491.6 (-4.1 %); long 16384/(91.18-45.51) = 358.7 vs 360.0 (+0.4 %). Engine summaries fcb 526.2/411.3 vs client 522.4/405.3 confirmed. bench_concurrency.py:102-103 returns t0/t1 per request and run_level (106-135) keeps only percentiles, so no steady-state window is computable today. One overstatement: the final round is not entirely stall-free - its own 16 admissions span 3.22 s (short) / 9.11 s (long) at its start (proto.log round-2 admission spans); the conclusion is unaffected because the marginal rate is measured, not modelled. The 4 % matters for the absolute headline in README.md:13 (522 vs vLLM 710) and CHANGELOG, not for A/B ratios. ROUNDS is already an env knob (ab_quick.sh:11), so the cheap fix is a protocol default.
- **Materiality verifier: weakened** [low] Re-parsed results/batchadmit/qprobe/proto.log: short2 ramp 3.68 s = 11.1 % of 33.0 s, drain 0.12 s; long2 9.01 s = 20.0 %, 0.12 s; per-slot BADM->BDONE 15.41/14.35 s and 20.01/17.45 s; short4 rounds 15.30/15.29/15.35/14.52 s; marginal R4-R2 = 16384/(65.28-33.33) = 512.8 (+4.3 % over 491.6), long 358.7 (-0.3 %). Numbers hold, but the marginal is a single-run subtraction of two walls: at 0.4 % per wall it carries +-0.9 %, a quarter of the effect, and the probe ran T16-142fc behind a timestamp proxy, not the default. The lens itself concedes the A/B ratio distortion is <=0.5 pp, so only the absolute headline and the Strata-vs-vLLM comparison are affected. The cheap fix already exists: ROUNDS is an env knob (ab_quick.sh line 11, used with ROUNDS=4 MIX=128 in #53) and #51 already identified the lockstep artefact. The 'proper' time-based protocol is 1-2 h for a ~4 % headline correction. Not decision-changing -> low.
  - Corrected claim: The 2-round short headline is about 4 % (+-1 %, single-run estimate on T16-142fc) below the continuous-traffic rate; the long headline is unbiased; A/B ratios are affected by <=0.5 pp. Use the existing ROUNDS=4 knob (or report the R4-R2 marginal) only when quoting absolute numbers or comparing with vLLM; no change to lever A/Bs is required.

### [medium] Label and time_ns in the prompt make prompt length and greedy text differ between arms (167 -> 178 tokens by config-name length)
*key `prompt-depends-on-label-and-time` · risk · status: **confirmed**, reviewer confidence 0.8*

**Claim.** bench_concurrency.py embeds a.label and time.time_ns() in every prompt, so arms with longer config names read longer prompts (+6-11 tokens, ~+3-5 ms per admission at ~0.57 ms/token for 150-300-token reads) and all arms generate different greedy text. The admission-time bias is ~0.3-0.5 % of the short wall against the longer-named arm, i.e. the same order as the +1 % levers. The text difference does not affect 16-user decode (no MTP drafts in batch windows) but does confound the 1-user MTP decode comparison.

**Evidence.** bench_concurrency.py line 48: prompt = f"[request {uid} / {a.label} / {time.time_ns()}]...". sweep.jsonl prompt_tok_avg (short): ab-fc-T16-142 167, -T16-142fc 168, ab-fcb-T16-142fcb 169, ab-aux2-T16-142fca 170, ab-lv-T16-142fca-mtpb 172, ab-lv-T16-142fca-s122437/s122537 176, ab-pf1k-T16-142fca-pf1024 177, -pf1024f 178; long 1232-1243. Engine logs confirm: engine-ab-fcb-T16-142fcb.log short admissions mean 169.2 tokens [167-174], engine-ab-lv-T16-142fca-s122437.log 176.2 [174-181]. Read-time slope from TESTLOG #51: 143 tok 145 ms, 293 tok 230 ms (0.57 ms/token); 32 admissions x 3-5 ms = 0.1-0.16 s of a 31.5 s wall. No MTP in batch: engine summaries give rows/group-steps = 16351/4104 = 3.984 = 1 token per slot per window (Strata-142/docs/BATCHING.md line 117: 'a request in a slot decodes without MTP drafts'). 1-user acceptance differs by text: engine-ab-fcb-T16-142fcb.log 1453/2313 = 62.8 % vs -fca.log 1475/2257 = 65.4 % (same engine path, different prompts).

**Recommendation.** Build the prompt from uid and prompt_tok only (e.g. f"[request {uid} / {a.prompt_tok}]"), keep the label out; uids are already unique within a server lifetime (1_000_000*(level+1)+..., warmup 10_000_000+w) and the prompt-cache is not configured in the T16 configs ('0 reused' in every admission line). Afterwards assert prompt_tok_avg equality across arms in ab_quick's summary. 10 min, 0 GPU.

- **Facts verifier: confirmed** [low] bench_concurrency.py:48 embeds a.label and time.time_ns(); sweep.jsonl prompt_tok_avg rises monotonically with label length (167 -> 178) exactly as listed; engine logs give short admissions 169.2 tokens [167-174] (fcb) and 176.2 [174-181] (s122437). Acceptance sums reproduce (fcb 1453/2313 = 62.8 %, fca 1475/2257 = 65.4 %). Three corrections: (1) the measured cost of the extra 7 tokens is ~2.3 ms per admission (s122437 short reads 179.2 ms vs fca/aux2 176.9/177.0 ms at 169-170 tokens, 0.33 ms/token), i.e. ~0.23 % of the short wall - the low end of the claimed 0.3-0.5 %; (2) 'same engine path' is wrong for the fcb-vs-fca acceptance comparison: configs/T16-142fcb.json sets STRATA_SPLIT_MTP_BATCH=1, which changes the drafter's K/V (PROFILE-next-levers.md:320 'only the drafter K/V changes'), so drafts and acceptance may legitimately differ, and the sums come from n=5 solo requests whose per-request acceptance spans 62.6-69.6 %, so 2.6 pp is noise either way; (3) '0 reused in every admission line' is not literal - the solo->slot hand-off reuses the prefix ('170 tokens = 169 reused + 1 read'), though no cross-request reuse occurs. The lens could also have cited ab_quick.sh:2-3 ('an exact change gives the same tokens') as directly contradicted by line 48. Fix is cheap and removes a same-direction bias for sub-1 % levers.
  - Corrected claim: Label and time_ns in the prompt make prompt length track the config-name length (167 -> 178 tokens); the measured read-time cost is ~2.3 ms per admission (~0.2 % of the short wall), and all arms generate different text, which adds noise to 1-user MTP comparisons (which for fcb vs fca are additionally confounded by STRATA_SPLIT_MTP_BATCH itself).
- **Materiality verifier: confirmed** [medium] bench_concurrency.py line 48 embeds a.label and time.time_ns(); sweep.jsonl prompt_tok_avg (short) 167 (ab-fc-T16-142) .. 169 (fcb) .. 176 (s122437) .. 178 (pf1024f) as claimed. configs/T16-*.json contain neither --batch-mtp nor --prompt-cache, and Strata-142/docs/BATCHING.md line 116 says slots decode without MTP drafts, so the text difference indeed cannot touch 16-user decode; TESTLOG #51 slope (143 tok 145 ms, 293 tok 230 ms = 0.57 ms/tok) x 7 tokens x 32 admissions = 0.13 s = 0.4 % of a 31.5 s wall, systematic against the longer-named arm, i.e. the same order as the one-run threshold. The engine-level drivers already fix prompts (results/levers/*/run.sh: PF_STAMP=fixed 'identical prompts across arms'); only the server bench lacks it. Weak spot: the 1-user acceptance evidence (1453/2313 vs 1475/2257) sums 5 full requests per arm (66 of the 71 'drafts accepted' lines are 1-token GEN calls) whose per-request acceptance spans 60.7-67.3 %, and fcb's STRATA_SPLIT_MTP_BATCH changes the drafter K/V path, so the 2.6-pp gap is not attributable to text alone. Fix is 10 min/0 GPU but shifts prompt_tok_avg for all future rows, so one ~2 min re-run of the default is needed as the new reference.

### [low] T=0.7 (rows <= #46) vs greedy (>= #49): +0.6 % / +2.1 % baseline shift, not via MTP; the progress chain is bridged correctly but the headers still say T=0.7
*key `t07-to-greedy-switch` · inconsistency · status: **confirmed**, reviewer confidence 0.85*

**Claim.** The protocol change mid-series shifted the T16-142 baseline by +0.6 % (short) / +2.1 % (long). It cannot act through MTP acceptance at 16 users (batch windows carry no drafts), so the long shift is unexplained (argmax vs sampler kernel, time of day, or series_142's level order 1,8 before 16 vs ab_quick's single warmup). TESTLOG #49, README line 296 and CHANGELOG 0.10.0 correctly use the greedy re-measurement (470/349) as the baseline, so the chain is not mixed in the step claims; but the TESTLOG header (lines 4-5), README line 69 and the README summary table (467/342 next to greedy sections) still present T=0.7 as the load profile, and the 1-user figures across the two protocols (115.7 vs 123-131 tok/s) are not comparable because 1-user acceptance does differ (58-62.5 % vs 62-65 %).

**Evidence.** series_142.sh passes no --temperature (bench default 0.7, bench_concurrency.py line 151); ab_quick.sh passes --temperature 0. Client side: #46 T16-142 means 466.9/342.1 vs greedy ab-fc/ab-fc2 means 469.7/349.4. Engine side (batch summaries, rows/s): T=0.7 engine-strata-T16-142-short-r1..3 = 472.4/470.8/465.3, -long 347.7/348.4/345.6; greedy engine-ab-fc/fc2-T16-142 = 477.2/470.5 and 358.2/353.8. rows/step = 3.984 in all (1 token per slot per window, no drafts). 1-user acceptance from the same logs: T=0.7 841/1451 (58.0 %), 879/1406 (62.5 %), 858/1434 (59.8 %), 845/1446, 1161/1871, 1135/1845; greedy 292/456 (64.0 %), 282/454 (62.1 %), 1475/2257 (65.4 %), 1453/2313 (62.8 %). README line 58 states '61 % draft acceptance at temperature 0.7'.

**Recommendation.** Add one line to the TESTLOG header and README load-profile paragraph: 'temperature 0.7 up to #46, greedy from #49 (ab_quick); 1-user MTP numbers differ by protocol'. No re-measurement needed (5 min, 0 GPU).

- **Facts verifier: confirmed** [low] series_142.sh:7-10 passes no --temperature (bench default 0.7 at bench_concurrency.py:151); ab_quick.sh:11-13 passes --temperature 0. Client means #46 466.9/342.1 vs greedy (473.2+466.2)/2 = 469.7 and (351.6+347.2)/2 = 349.4 (+0.6 % / +2.1 %). Engine summaries reproduce: T=0.7 short 472.4/470.8/465.3, long 347.7/348.4/345.6; greedy 477.2/470.5 and 358.2/353.8 rows/s; rows/group-steps 16351/4104 = 3.98 everywhere (one token per slot per window, Strata-142/docs/BATCHING.md:117). Acceptance figures all reproduce (841/1451, 879/1406, 858/1434, 845/1446, 1161/1871, 1135/1845; greedy 292/456, 282/454, 1475/2257, 1453/2313). TESTLOG.md:4-5, README.md:58 and :69 still say temperature 0.7; README.md:296 and CHANGELOG 0.10.0 correctly use the greedy 470/349 baseline. Note the T=0.7 series also ran levels 1,8,16,24 on one server, so the protocol change is more than temperature; the one-line documentation fix is the right response.
- **Materiality verifier: confirmed** [low] series_142.sh passes no --temperature (default 0.7, bench_concurrency.py line 151), ab_quick.sh passes --temperature 0. #46 means 466.9/342.1 vs greedy ab-fc/ab-fc2 means 469.7/349.4 = +0.6 %/+2.1 %; engine rows/s match (472.4/470.8/465.3 vs 477.2/470.5; 347.7/348.4/345.6 vs 358.2/353.8). TESTLOG lines 4-5, README line 69 and upstream/ISSUE.md line 7 still state temperature 0.7; README line 281 table (467/342) is correctly labelled '3 repetitions, TESTLOG #46' and #49/CHANGELOG 0.10.0 use the greedy 470/349, so the step chain is not mixed. The +2.1 % long shift is ~3 se but confounded by level order (series_142 ran 1,8 before 16) and time of day; no decision depends on it. Doc-only, 5 min.

### [medium] No GPU clock/temperature/power record exists for any run; the long arm always runs on hot GPUs
*key `no-gpu-clock-temperature-logging` · gap · status: **weakened**, reviewer confidence 0.9*

**Claim.** Nothing in linux/*.sh logs nvidia-smi clocks, temperature or power during a benchmark, and no such record exists under results/. The 3090s are power-limited, so boost clock drifts with temperature; this is a plausible contributor to the 0.4-0.8 % per-run noise and the T16-142 outliers, and it is unverifiable today. Within an arm the long run always follows the short run (hot GPUs) - the same for every arm, so no between-arm bias, but a short-vs-long comparison carries it.

**Evidence.** grep nvidia-smi linux/*.sh: no hits; the only use is results/batchadmit/qprobe/run.sh line 6 as a 'GPUs busy' guard. grep -rl 'clocks.sm|temperature.gpu|power.draw|MHz' results/: no files. run_strata_variant.sh line 28 saves /v1/status once at start (status-ab-fcb-T16-142fcb-short.json: uptime_s 3, machine.gpu temp_c 56, power_w 453 at idle); serve/server.py lines 3330-3334 expose temp/power/util from NVML but only on request. ab_quick.sh line 11 'tail -2' also drops the 'ready after N s' line, so model-load time per arm is not recorded either.

**Recommendation.** In run_strata_variant.sh start `nvidia-smi --query-gpu=timestamp,index,clocks.sm,clocks.mem,temperature.gpu,power.draw,clocks_throttle_reasons.active --format=csv -lms 1000 > results/gpu-$LABEL.csv &` and kill it after the bench; print mean SM clock per GPU next to tok/s in the ab summary (zero GPU cost, ~10 min). If arms differ by >2 % in mean clock, add a fixed cool-down before each arm or lock clocks (`nvidia-smi -lgc`, root). Keep the 'ready after' line in the .out files.

- **Facts verifier: weakened** [low] True that no linux/*.sh logs nvidia-smi telemetry and no clocks/temperature/power time series exists under results/ (grep for clocks.sm|temperature.gpu|power.draw|MHz: no files). Inaccuracies: the 'GPUs busy' guard is not only in qprobe/run.sh:6 but in ~20 run.sh/drive.sh files (e.g. results/fixedcost/final/run.sh:14, results/levers/mmq-sort/run.sh:4); and a per-arm pre-run snapshot does exist - status-*.json machine.gpu temp_c/power_w (fcb 56 C/453 W, fca 60 C/463 W, s122437 47 C/440 W at ready; serve/server.py:3330-3334) - just no clocks and nothing during the run. The attribution to the T16-142 outliers is not supported by the data: those runs lost 4-6 % on admission reads and <=1 % on decode (GPU3 is 98 % busy in decode, so a clock drop would hit decode at least as much). The recommendation needs a caveat the lens missed: Strata-142/src/core/verify.cpp:703-705 records that an NVML query (nvidia-smi) between requests stalled a stage's graph on 2x RTX 3090 (#905) when STRATA_DF_BRANCH=1; it is off by default and set in no linux config, so 1 s polling is safe only while that stays off. Cheap and harmless to add, but not a likely explanation of anything seen so far.
  - Corrected claim: No GPU clock/temperature/power time series is recorded during any run; only a one-shot temp/power snapshot per arm exists in status-*.json. The T16-142 outliers show a prefill-side pattern (reads +4-6 %, decode <=1 %) that does not look like clock drift. Adding nvidia-smi polling is safe with STRATA_DF_BRANCH unset (verify.cpp:703-705, #905).
- **Materiality verifier: weakened** [low] Verified: grep nvidia-smi over linux/*.sh finds nothing; results/ has no clocks.sm/temperature.gpu/power.draw/MHz records; nvidia-smi appears only as a 'GPUs busy' guard in results/*/run.sh; status-ab-fcb-T16-142fcb-short.json holds one sample at uptime 3 s (temp_c 56, power_w 453 aggregate). The gap is real, but its materiality is speculative: the fc-family repeats agree to 0.06-0.5 % across more than an hour (00:34 -> 00:38, 01:33 -> 02:48), which bounds any thermal effect on A/B ratios at about 0.5 %, and the one T16-142 outlier (r3 462.5) coincides with TTFT p50 doubling (0.73 -> 1.41 s), an admission-queue event rather than clocks. ### [low] server.py and the 16-thread client run unpinned next to an engine that occupies CPUs 0-63 and 65-127; no evidence of harm, cheap to pin
*key `client-and-server-share-engine-host` · risk · status: **weakened**, reviewer confidence 0.5*

**Claim.** The engine pins the host thread to CPU 0, 63 pool workers to CPUs 1-63 and (with --aux-cpus 65-127) its helper threads to 65-127, leaving only CPU 64 unclaimed. server.py (relays 16 SSE streams) and bench_concurrency.py (16 threads) are unpinned and can be scheduled onto worker cores (which spin with _mm_pause before sleeping) or CPU 0. The data shows no interference (fc-family repeats agree to 0.06-0.5 %, and the client-vs-engine overhead is identical across arms), so this is a hygiene item, not a demonstrated bias.

**Evidence.** engine-ab-fcb-T16-142fcb.log (segment after the last 'engine started'): '63 pool workers on logical processors 1,...,63, the host thread on 0'; 'strata aux cpus: ... go to CPUs 65,...,127'. Strata-142/src/kernels/cpu/pool.cpp lines 499-520 (park with _mm_pause, sleep after kSpinBeforeSleep). Strata-142/serve/server.py line 662 pins only replica engines, not itself. run_strata_variant.sh / ab_quick.sh: no taskset. Overhead client vs engine summary: fcb 522.4 vs 526.2 rows/s (0.7 %) short, 405.3 vs 411.3 (1.5 %) long; fca 490.5 vs 494.0 (0.7 %), 377.6 vs 383.1 (1.5 %).

**Recommendation.** Wrap server.py and the client in `taskset -c 32-63` (or 64 plus a few worker cores) in run_strata_variant.sh and record Cpus_allowed_list of both PIDs into results/ (10 min, 0 GPU). Low priority; do it together with the telemetry change.

- **Facts verifier: weakened** [low] Facts verified: engine log '63 pool workers on logical processors 1..63, the host thread on 0' and 'aux cpus ... 65..127 (on; 9 threads placed so far)'; pool.cpp:499-520 spins with _mm_pause before sleeping; serve/server.py:662 pins only replica engines; no taskset in run_strata_variant.sh/ab_quick.sh; client-vs-engine overhead 0.7 %/1.5 % identical in fcb and fca; host is a Threadripper 3990X, 128 logical CPUs, 1 NUMA node. The recommendation is counterproductive as written: 'taskset -c 32-63' would place server.py and the 16 client threads on pool-worker CPUs (1-63), which spin; the only unclaimed logical CPU is 64 (SMT sibling of the host's CPU 0) and 65-127 carry just 9 aux threads, so a slice of 65-127 or CPU 64 is the sensible target. No harm demonstrated, so hygiene only.
  - Corrected claim: server.py and the client are unpinned next to an engine occupying CPUs 0-63 and 65-127; no interference is visible (fc-family repeats within 0.06-0.5 %). If pinning, use CPU 64 or a slice of 65-127 (shared with 9 aux threads), never worker CPUs 1-63.
- **Materiality verifier: weakened** [low] Engine log (last segment of engine-ab-fcb-T16-142fcb.log): '63 pool workers on logical processors 1..63, the host thread on 0', aux threads on 65..127; Strata-142/serve/server.py lines 662-663 pin only replica engines; no taskset in run_strata_variant.sh/ab_quick.sh; pool.cpp 497-525 spin with _mm_pause then sleep. Claim accurate, but the lens itself documents no harm (client-vs-engine overhead a constant 0.7 %/1.5 %; fc repeats 0.06-0.5 %). The suggested taskset -c 32-63 would put Python on spinning worker cores, not obviously better than the scheduler's choice, and with all experts resident the pool is mostly parked. Nothing changes until a bias is shown; fold into any later telemetry edit.
  - Corrected claim: server.py and the client are unpinned next to a fully pinned engine; the recorded data show no interference, so pinning is optional hygiene with no expected effect on decisions.

Correctly stated; printing the engine summary line in ab_quick is a 5-min nicety.

**Done well (this lens):**
- Exactness is established with byte-wise token dumps (batch_test16/32, interleave, overlap incl. RESUME), never inferred from the benchmark; greedy in the speed A/B is the right complement.
- Env-switch arms share one binary (strata-g3-142fc for fc/fca/fcb), so the A/B isolates the switch rather than a rebuild.
- Fresh server per arm, arms back-to-back in one invocation, and #57 re-measured the reference back-to-back after #56 had used an hour-old one.
- TESTLOG #49 re-measured the T16-142 baseline under greedy (470/349) instead of chaining the T=0.7 #46 numbers; README line 296 and CHANGELOG 0.10.0 use the same greedy baseline.
- The lockstep artifact of all-512-token replies was recognised (#51, #53) and a mixed-length mode (MIX=128, ROUNDS=4) exists as the realistic-traffic reference.
- The engine's own 'rows/s (admissions included)' summary agrees with the client numbers within a constant 0.7 % / 1.5 % across arms, so the client/HTTP path adds no between-arm bias.
- Warmup is sufficient: the 2.5x first-request read penalty is absorbed by --warmup 1, the long run on the warm server shows no first-request effect, and graph-capture counts are identical in both arms.
- qprobe short2/short4/long2/long4 on one server already provide the data to quantify ramp and steady state (used above).

**Points the verifiers added:**
- Documented short-prompt length is wrong: TESTLOG.md:5 and README.md:69 say 'short ≈ 120' prompt tokens, but every short row in results/sweep.jsonl has prompt_tok_avg 162-178 (e.g. ab-fcb-T16-142fcb-short 169; bench_concurrency.py:47 gives FILLER x1 for --prompt-tok 100 ≈ 60 tokens + header/task), so the HTTP benchmark reads ~165-175 tokens, matching the engine-level '150-token read' scale.
- The slow T16-142 runs are a detectable prefill-side disturbance, not generic noise: batch-admission reads 217.0 vs 204.7 ms (r3 vs r1/r2) and 213.7 vs 205.2 ms (ab-fc2 vs ab-fc), TTFT p50 1.41/1.09 vs 0.73/0.81 s, decode p50 within 1 %; within-rep paired overlap-series differences are 4.1/4.1/3.8 tok/s. A plausibility check on the engine log already copied per arm (admission read time or TTFT p50 within ~15 % of the partner arm) would flag such runs at zero GPU cost, cheaper than tripling runs.
- TESTLOG #56's tie-break 12,24,37 over 12,25,37 rests on +0.4 %/+0.5 % single runs (519.2/398.0 vs 517.2/396.0; engine 523.2/405.7 vs 521.1/403.8 rows/s), below the lens's own 1.2 % threshold; the predicted 8-10 ms 1.2K-read advantage of 12,25,37 (PROFILE-next-levers.md:319) did not appear (long admission reads 471.4 vs 473.4 ms). This is the one default-relevant decision actually below resolution, and the lens did not name it.
- The current default T16-142fcb has never been measured under the mixed-reply-length protocol that TESTLOG #53 declared the 'new reference for realistic traffic': sweep.jsonl MIX rows (387-390) exist only for T16-142fca and fca-overlap; all #54-#57 decisions are lockstep-only, where 336 of 340 Strata/ab rows have every reply at exactly max_tokens.
- ab_quick.sh:2-3 states 'an exact change gives the same tokens, so only the speed differs', but bench_concurrency.py:48 puts label and time_ns into every prompt, so two arms never see identical prompts and token equality is not even checkable from the benchmark; exactness rests entirely on batch_test16/overlap/interleave dumps (which is fine) and the comment should say so.
- The project's own rule 'three repetitions only to confirm a new default' (TESTLOG 'Open / next steps', ab_quick.sh line 4) was not applied to the last three defaults: T16-142fc (#49: 2 runs), T16-142fca (#52: 1 run, plus the fca arm of #57 = 2), T16-142fcb (#57: 1 run). CHANGELOG 0.12.0 and the README quote 522/405 from a single run, i.e. +-1-1.5 % at 95 %. Two more fcb runs cost ~4 min GPU by the measured arm time and would also give the current family its own noise estimate.
- Per-arm GPU cost is ~2 min, not 4-5 min: file timestamps status-ab-fcb-T16-142fcb-short.json 02:45:42 -> ab-fcb-T16-142fcb-long.txt 02:47:24 -> next status 02:47:59 (incl. ~30 s load); ab-lv arms 02:38:42 -> 02:40:03 -> 02:40:38. Every 'repeat' recommendation (and ab_quick.sh line 2) overstates the cost by 2-2.5x, which changes the proportionality judgement in favour of 3-pair runs for small levers.
- Two sub-noise picks are recorded as 'better' without an equivalence note: #52 65-127 vs 65-79 (492.9/376.2 vs 491.2/375.6 = +0.35 %/+0.16 %) and #56 12,24,37 vs 12,25,37 (519.2/398.0 vs 517.2/396.0 = +0.4 %/+0.5 %). Both choices are harmless, but marking them 'equivalent within noise' in the TESTLOG prevents re-testing the loser later.
- sweep.jsonl rows carry no timestamp (bench_concurrency.py lines 126-135), so time-of-day/thermal drift and arm order can only be reconstructed from file mtimes; adding one 'time' field (1 line) makes the lens's drift questions answerable from the data instead of the filesystem.


## Lens: workload

### [high] The MIX=128 'realistic reference' (410/290) mixes a real workload change with a closed-loop drain tail; 'lockstep costs ~20 %' is not a valid reading
*key `mix-reference-conflates-three-effects` · inconsistency · status: **weakened**, reviewer confidence 0.85*

**Claim.** Most of the MIX-vs-fixed drop is not lockstep at all: switching to 128-512-token replies raises admissions per output token by 1.6x (64 reads for 20,258 tokens vs 32 for 16,384), which is a legitimate prefill/decode-mix change, and a further ~6 % is the closed-loop tail where early-finishing users idle. In fixed mode the engine's group windows are 99.6 % full, so lockstep bursts cost throughput almost nothing; they only inflate TTFT (3 requests queue behind each admission, p50 1.0 s vs 0.21 s in MIX).

**Evidence.** Engine stat lines (last session) in /media/raid0/StrataQ38FN/linux/results/engine-ab-aux2-T16-142fca*.log: fixed short 16351 rows / 4104 group-steps in 32.944 s (496.3 rows/s), long 42.549 s (384.3); engine-ab-mix-T16-142fca*.log: 20151 rows / 5689 steps in 48.778 s (413.1), long 5684 steps in 68.589 s (293.8). Rows per group-step: fixed 3.984 (99.6 % of 4), MIX 3.542 (88.6 %). Calibrating the per-admission stall from the fixed runs with the pure-decode rate 616.6 rows/s (results/profile2/dec_rows.txt) gives 0.201 s (short) / 0.501 s (long); predicting MIX with 64 admissions and full occupancy gives 442.6 / 311.2 rows/s, i.e. -10.8 % / -19.0 % from the admission share alone; the observed 413.1 / 293.8 leaves -6.7 % / -5.6 % for the tail + partial windows. Per-user token totals under MIX=128 ROUNDS=4 (bench_concurrency.py line 53, uid = 1,000,000 + 4i + r) range 1025..1539, mean 1266 (mean/max 0.823), so the slowest user sets the wall. TESTLOG #53 records 409.8/290.3 as 'new reference for realistic traffic'; agg_total_tps (prompt+output) in results/sweep.jsonl is actually higher for MIX long (1422.4 vs 1283.7), i.e. the engine did more work, just more of it was prefill.

**Recommendation.** (1) Make the MIX mode tail-free: give every user the same multiset of reply lengths in a per-user rotation (e.g. 4 lengths summing to a constant, rotated by uid) so requests still vary but all users finish together: ~3 lines in one_request(); or compute agg_out_tps over the steady-state window only (tokens emitted between 'all users started' and 'first user finished', interpolated per request): ~20 lines. (2) Re-run MIX for T16-142fcb and T16-142fcb+overlap: 2 arms x ~5 min GPU. (3) Reword TESTLOG #53/CHANGELOG 0.11.0: the 410/290 is 'shorter replies + tail', not 'lockstep'. Total ~1 h of work, ~12 GPU minutes.

- **Facts verifier: confirmed** [medium] Every number recomputes exactly: engine-ab-aux2-T16-142fca.log last session lines 109244/109409 (4104 steps, 16351 rows, 32.944 s = 496.3; 42.549 s = 384.3), engine-ab-mix-T16-142fca.log lines 109943/110273 (5689/5684 steps, 20151 rows, 48.778/68.589 s = 413.1/293.8); rows/step 3.984 vs 3.542; per-user MIX totals from bench_concurrency.py line 53 with uid=1,000,000+4i+r are 1025..1539 (sum 20258 = out_tok_total of sweep rows 387-390, mean 1266, mean/max 0.823); stall calibration 0.201/0.501 s against 616.6 rows/s gives 442.6/311.2 predicted, residual -6.7/-5.6 %; agg_total_tps 1422.4 vs 1283.7 (rows 388/386). Two caveats: (a) no document actually says 'lockstep costs ~20 %' - TESTLOG #53 and DESIGN-batched-admission.md line 420 attribute only the queue depth k to lockstep, so rec (3) shrinks to a one-line clarification; (b) the MIX figure is consumed: upstream/PROFILE-next-levers.md lines 285-286 and 317 fit the 'mixed traffic' lever column to #53 ('effective D of about 537'), so the ~6 % tail artifact propagates into the lever projections. Recs (1) tail-free MIX and (2) re-run on T16-142fcb (MIX was only run on T16-142fca) are actionable.

### [high] No open-loop (Poisson) mode exists; the overlap-as-default question cannot be settled by the closed loop
*key `no-open-loop-mode` · gap · status: **weakened**, reviewer confidence 0.85*

**Claim.** bench_concurrency.py, upstream's tools/serve_load.py and ab_quick.sh are all closed-loop at users == slots, i.e. permanently saturated. They cannot produce a latency-vs-load curve or a max-sustainable-rate figure, and the overlap's p95 penalty (+20-25 % at 16 users, #41/#46) is largely the closed loop's own artifact: #44 notes 'in a closed loop someone almost always waits'. At a realistic arrival rate below saturation a read beside decoding rarely has another read queued behind it, so the p95 penalty may vanish while the p50 gain (-55/-65 %) remains; near saturation the closed-loop picture returns. Only an open-loop sweep shows where the crossover is.

**Evidence.** /media/raid0/StrataQ38FN/linux/bench_concurrency.py lines 106-117 (ThreadPoolExecutor with n users, each sending rounds back to back; no arrival process); /media/raid0/StrataQ38FN/Strata-142/tools/serve_load.py docstring ('sends C streamed chat requests at the same moment'); no 'poisson/arrival/request rate' in Strata-142/docs or tools (grep). TESTLOG #41/#44/#46/#53: overlap verdict depends on queue depth (fixed mode: p50 -73 %, p95 +20-25 %; MIX mode: p50 worse, throughput +2/+6 %) - opposite signs under two closed-loop variants. An open-loop generator is already installed: /home/administrator/G3_VLLM/venv/lib/python3.12/site-packages/vllm/benchmarks/serve.py has --request-rate and --burstiness (lines 376-423), --backend/--base-url/--endpoint (1617-1633), --extra-body (1976), --dataset-name custom (datasets/datasets.py line 1612), so `vllm bench serve` can target Strata's /v1/chat/completions. Caveats: it needs an HF-format --tokenizer (the Strata pack's tokenizer dir is vocab.json/merges.txt only; any local Qwen3 tokenizer dir gives close counts); thinking must be turned off via --extra-body '{"reasoning_effort":"none"}' or the config key "reasoning_effort" (server.py line 2742); Strata ignores ignore_eos (0 matches in serve/server.py).

**Recommendation.** Either add `--rate R --duration S` to bench_concurrency.py (one thread per Poisson arrival with exponential gaps, report tok/s over the measurement window plus TTFT/e2e p50/p95 per rate; ~60 lines, 1-2 h) or use the installed `vllm bench serve` with a natural-text custom JSONL. Sweep rates 0.5 / 1 / 2 / 4 req/s (saturation is about 522/512 ~ 1 req/s for 512-token replies) for T16-142fcb with and without STRATA_PIPE_OVERLAP=1: ~2 min per point, ~16 GPU minutes. Decide the overlap default from the rate band the server actually sees, not from the saturated loop.

- **Facts verifier: weakened** [medium] Code facts hold: bench_concurrency.py lines 106-117 (ThreadPoolExecutor, back-to-back rounds), Strata-142/tools/serve_load.py docstring ('at the same moment'), no poisson/arrival/rate in Strata-142/docs or tools, vllm/benchmarks/serve.py --request-rate/--burstiness (argparse at lines 1716/1725; 376-423 is the get_request docstring), datasets/datasets.py line 1612 'custom', server.py line 2742 config_effort, 0 matches for ignore_eos. But the predicted outcome is contradicted by data already in the repo: with little queueing (MIX, #53) the overlap made TTFT p50 WORSE (0.21 -> 0.34 s short, 0.54 -> 1.35 s long, sweep rows 387-390) because the overlapped read itself is longer - engine-ab-mix-T16-142fca.log 64 short reads mean 168 ms / 64 long reads mean 483 ms vs engine-ab-mix-T16-142fca-overlap*.log 251-256 ms for 163-169 tokens and 757-763 ms for 1.2K. So at a low arrival rate the fixed-loop p50 gain (pure queue removal) does not remain and the p95 penalty does not vanish, it becomes the +50 % longer read. The two closed-loop variants already bracket both regimes; an open-loop generator would add a latency-vs-load curve for capacity planning, not settle the overlap default.
  - Corrected claim: An open-loop (Poisson) mode is indeed missing from all three generators, but it would not flip the overlap default: #53 already shows that with little queueing the overlap raises TTFT p50 (+62 % short, +150 % long) because an overlapped read is ~50 % longer (168 -> 251-256 ms, 483 -> 757-763 ms). Its value is a latency-vs-load / max-sustainable-rate curve for deployment planning, which no current tool produces.
- **Materiality verifier: weakened** [medium] Evidence verified: bench_concurrency.py run_level (lines 106-117) is a ThreadPoolExecutor of n users sending rounds back to back; Strata-142/tools/serve_load.py docstring 'sends C streamed chat requests at the same moment'; grep for poisson/arrival/request-rate over Strata-142/docs, tools and linux/ finds only the design doc's queue simulation (DESIGN-batched-admission.md 187/421) and an unrelated 'payload-arrival' in A770_SPEED_SPEC.md. vLLM venv serve.py has --request-rate/--burstiness (376-423), --backend/--base-url/--endpoint (1617-1633), --extra-body (~1976), custom dataset (datasets/datasets.py 1612). Small correction: the pack tokenizer dir also holds tokenizer.json, not only vocab/merges. Materiality is overstated for a throughput project: the overlap is throughput-neutral at 16 users (#41/#46/#49) and +2/+6 % in MIX, so an open-loop sweep refines the opt-in latency guidance and the 'fix the p95 tail' next step, not the throughput default. The claim that the p95 penalty 'may vanish' is a hypothesis: #44's 8-user closed-loop run (sweep rows 310-313) still shows p95 +19 % (2.60->3.10, 5.49->6.56) with 8 free slots, i.e. the penalty is burst-driven; only an open loop can show whether bursts occur at realistic rates. Cost (~20 GPU min incl. 2 server starts, 1-2 h scripting) is proportionate.
  - Corrected claim: No open-loop mode exists (verified). It would settle when to recommend STRATA_PIPE_OVERLAP=1 for interactive deployments and whether the planned 'p95 tail' work is needed, but it cannot change the throughput default, so it is a medium-priority deployment question, not a high-severity gap.

### [medium] The 1.2K benchmark prompt is one 72-token paragraph repeated 16 times (61 distinct token ids); expert-count figures in the test output are synthetic, not measured
*key `filler-prompt-not-natural-text` · risk · status: **weakened**, reviewer confidence 0.75*

**Claim.** The prompt that decides prefill-side defaults has almost no lexical diversity, so MoE routing skew per 512-token chunk and PLE n-gram locality differ from natural text. The '147 experts (150-token prompt)' and '219 experts (512-token chunk)' lines are hard-coded test shapes, not routing measured on either prompt. Indirect evidence suggests the effect on read time is small (<= ~3-4 %), but prefill levers whose gain depends on the per-expert row-count distribution (MMQ group sort: 'one hot expert sets J for its whole 16-expert group') are being judged through ab_quick on this filler.

**Evidence.** Tokenised with Strata-142/tools/strata_tokenizer.py and the pack tokenizer: FILLER = 72 tokens; FILLER*16 = 1,137 tokens with 61 distinct token ids and only 73 distinct 4-grams over 1,134 positions (bench_concurrency.py lines 28-33, 47: doc = FILLER * (prompt_tok // 60)). The 147/219 figures come from /media/raid0/StrataQ38FN/Strata-142/tests/cuda/prefill_mmq_tab_test.cu lines 611-612: order_case(..., skewed_counts(147, 1, 40, rng), ..., "150-token prompt") and skewed_counts(219, 1, 160, rng) for "512-token chunk" - synthetic. The engine-level work deliberately used natural text: results/batchadmit/scaling/scale_driver.py docstring ('slices of the repo's Markdown corpus (diverse routing, unlike a repeated filler)') and results/levers/mmq-sort/pf_driver.py lines 46-47 (Strata-142/**/*.md). Read-time comparison on the same config (T16-142fca): HTTP filler 1,235 tok read in 485 ms mean over 32 reads (engine-ab-aux2-T16-142fca.log; includes the ~28 ms merged first window per results/fixedcost/final) vs natural text 1,193 tok in 456.6-458.3 ms (results/profile2/pf_reads_plain.txt, read excludes that window) - about equal per token, filler at most ~3 % faster. Decode is unaffected (generated text is natural; batch slots run no drafts).

**Recommendation.** Add `--corpus FILE` to bench_concurrency.py: slice a natural-text file at a uid-seeded offset to ~prompt_tok tokens (reuse the Strata-142 Markdown corpus or eval/data/sets.json questions), keep the unique prefix; ~20 lines. Run one ab_quick A/B FILLER vs corpus on T16-142fcb (2 arms, ~10 GPU minutes). If the difference exceeds the ~1.5 % run-to-run spread, make corpus the default and re-baseline (keep FILLER rows for history). At minimum use the corpus for the pending prefill-lever A/Bs (MMQ group sort, self-commit).

### [medium] Greedy inflates the 16-user headline by at most ~2 % (batch slots run no MTP drafts) but the docs mix greedy and T=0.7 numbers without saying so
*key `greedy-headline-labelling` · inconsistency · status: **confirmed**, reviewer confidence 0.85*

**Claim.** The premise that MTP acceptance makes 522/405 a greedy-inflated number is wrong for the batch path: slots decode one row per token with no drafts, so acceptance is irrelevant there; the measured greedy premium on the batch path is +0.6 % short / +2.1 % long. What does mix is the documentation: README/TESTLOG still describe the load profile as temperature 0.7 while every number from #49 on is greedy, the single-user '~121-131 tok/s (MTP)' spans a T=0.7 and a greedy measurement, and the summary table compares greedy Strata (522/405) with T=0.7 vLLM (710/400). Note that Strata's server treats an absent temperature as greedy, so greedy is representative for chat clients that omit it.

**Evidence.** No drafts in batch decode: engine stat '16351 rows' for 16,384 output tokens (1 row/token) in engine-ab-fcb-T16-142fcb-short.log; every BGEN admission logs 'drafts accepted 0 of 0'; Strata-142/docs/BATCHING.md: slots decode without MTP drafts unless --batch-mtp, which the pipelined 4-group split turns off. Greedy premium: T16-142 at 16 users greedy 469.7/349.4 (mean of ab-fc and ab-fc2 rows in results/sweep.jsonl) vs T=0.7 466.9/342.1 (strata-T16-142-*-r1..r3, series_142.out) = +0.6 % / +2.1 %. Single user: #1 121 tok/s at 0.7 (0.1.41), #57 130.9 greedy; README line 11 '~121-131 tok/s (MTP)'. README line 69 and TESTLOG line 5 say 'temperature 0.7'; ab_quick.sh line 11-13 passes --temperature 0; CHANGELOG 0.9.0 and TESTLOG 'Open / next steps' do say greedy. vLLM rows vllm031-flashnext-pc-* (#12) were T=0.7. Server default: Strata-142/serve/server.py lines 589-590 ('An absent temperature keeps the engine's default, which is greedy'), sampling_defaults_from_config line 5873 (no 'sampling' block in configs/T16-142fcb.json).

**Recommendation.** Label greedy in README (summary table, lines 11-13, 69, 314) and the TESTLOG header (~10 min). Optionally run T16-142fcb once at T=0.7 with 3 reps (ab_quick with --temperature 0.7 patched in, ~15 GPU minutes) to close the #1-#46 series with one comparable point; low priority since the batch path has no drafts and the premium is bounded by the +0.6/+2.1 % already measured on T16-142.

- **Facts verifier: confirmed** [low] Greedy premium recomputed exactly: ab-fc/ab-fc2 T16-142 (sweep rows 367/368/375/376) mean 469.7/349.4 vs strata-T16-142-*-r1..r3 at 16 users (rows 315,331,347 / 319,335,351) mean 466.9/342.1 = +0.6 %/+2.1 %. One evidence item is misread: the 'drafts accepted 0 of 0' lines are the per-admission summary of the prompt read plus the one merged first token ('prompt 169 tokens ... read in 194 ms, 1 generated in 13 ms', engine-ab-fcb-T16-142fcb-short.log), so they say nothing about the batch decode; the valid evidence is 16351 rows for 16,384 tokens (one row per token) and BATCHING.md line 37 (pipelined path runs no slot drafts). The vLLM rows' temperature is not recorded anywhere (no vllm/*.sh passes --temperature; bench_concurrency default 0.7), so 'vLLM at 0.7' is plausible but unverifiable.

Greedy premium from the right rows: ab-fc/ab-fc2 T16-142 (473.2+466.2)/2 = 469.7 and (351.6+347.2)/2 = 349.4 vs series_142 T=0.7 r1-r3 (469.9+468.3+462.5)/3 = 466.9 and (342.5+343.2+340.5)/3 = 342.1, same prompt length (168/1233) -> +0.6 % / +2.1 %, cross-day and inside the ~1.5 % spread, so it is an upper bound. The labelling fix is real and costs 10 minutes without GPU; the optional T=0.7 run would not change anything (premium bounded, batch slots run no drafts) and should be skipped.

### [medium] The multi-turn/agent path was last measured on the 0.1.41 engine (#38); five defaults later nothing covers the traffic shape real chat clients produce
*key `agent-multiturn-path-stale` · gap · status: **confirmed**, reviewer confidence 0.85*

**Claim.** All defaults since #45 (0.1.42 rebase, fixed-cost switches, --aux-cpus, split 12,24,37, MTP batch) were decided on fresh single-turn requests. The agent config README recommends is two engines old, lacks every later default, and the throughput default itself (32K context, no conversation-cache flags) has never been measured under multi-turn load, although Open WebUI/agent clients resend growing histories every turn. The continuation-from-slot path those clients use has a known open divergence from a fresh solo read (row after #40), and the fixed-cost switches' exactness runs did not include --kv-resident/conversation-cache flags.

TESTLOG #48 exactness: batch_test16, batch_overlap_test, interleave - none with --kv-resident; patch 0014 refuses the overlap under --kv-resident (patches/v0.1.42/0014 line 552). Open issue row after #40: next turn from a batch-decoded slot differs at token 15-54 from a fresh solo read (results/overlap/exactness-r1/soak2-ref*.out). configs/T16-142fcb.json: --max-context 32768, no --conversation-cache-*/--kv-resident; BATCHING.md: a finished slot keeps its conversation, so only 16 conversations are cached on the default.

**Recommendation.** Create configs/X16-agent-142fcb.json = T16-142fcb args+env plus --max-context 262144 --kv-resident 32768 --conversation-cache-mib 24576 --conversation-cache-slots 24 --prompt-cache 24 --prompt-cache-every 8192 and STRATA_KV_PREFETCH=1 (check GPU3 VRAM with the 12,24,37 split). Update README lines 14, 49-60 and the agent table. ~25 GPU minutes, ~1 h total.

**Recommendation.** State ~1.5 % as the single-run decision threshold in ab_quick.sh/TESTLOG; for deltas below it run the arm twice (adds ~5 min). No GPU cost beyond that.

### [low] Sixteen fixed TOPICS under greedy are fine for A/B and a mild generality caveat only
*key `fixed-topics-greedy-ok` · confirmation · status: **weakened**, reviewer confidence 0.8*

**Claim.** Every run generating the same 16 (x2) texts is a strength for exact A/Bs (identical token streams, only speed differs). For generality the only content-dependent cost in batch decode is the routed-expert union per 4-row window, second order with all experts resident and a fixed-cost-dominated window; MTP acceptance plays no role because slots run no drafts. All prompts are English technical prose and every reply is forced to exactly 512 tokens, so EOS-terminated or German traffic is simply not represented.

**Evidence.** bench_concurrency.py lines 19-27 (TOPICS), 46 (topic = uid % 16), 48-50 ('Do not stop early'); out_tok_total = 16384 = 32 x 512 in every fixed-mode row of results/sweep.jsonl (no early stop). Profile: window ~6.5 ms, +1.34 ms per row on GPU3, 85 % GPU (PROFILE-next-levers.md). bench_multiturn.py lines 14-27 builds documents from a 40-word random vocabulary (not natural text either).

**Recommendation.** No change for A/B. If the corpus option from the filler finding is built, let it also supply the topics/tasks (and a German share) so one flag covers both; no extra GPU cost.

The load shape (fixed 512 length, fixed topics) is identical, which is what matters for throughput; exactness is proven by the engine tests, not by ab_quick (its line 2-3 comment overstates this too). Minor: bench_multiturn.py WORDS has 42 entries, not 40.

### [low] The headline assumes thinking off and 512-token replies; state it, because thinking-on traffic is decode-bound and shifts which levers matter
*key `thinking-off-512-cap-assumption` · suggestion · status: **weakened**, reviewer confidence 0.7*

**Claim.** With reasoning_effort none and max_tokens 512 the benchmark's output/prompt ratio is fixed (3:1 short, 0.4:1 long). Qwen3.8 clients that leave thinking on produce multi-thousand-token outputs, making the 16-slot decode ceiling (~617 rows/s) the cap and prefill levers (aux-cpus, MTP batch, group sort) nearly irrelevant, while the split change matters more.

**Recommendation.** One sentence in README's summary and the TESTLOG header; optionally one ab_quick run with --max-tok 2048 on T16-142fcb (~8 GPU minutes) to publish the decode-bound figure.

**Done well (this lens):**
- Engine-level profiling and the batched-admission scaling deliberately used natural Markdown text and say why (results/batchadmit/scaling/scale_driver.py docstring; results/levers/mmq-sort/pf_driver.py) - the filler concern is confined to the HTTP benchmark.
- Lockstep was diagnosed directly with the waiting=N probe (TESTLOG #51, results/batchadmit/qprobe: 'k=4 is a lockstep artifact of the benchmark') and the batched-admission design was dropped on that basis instead of being built.
- A mixed-length mode (--max-tok-min / MIX=128) exists and is deterministic per request id, so both arms of an A/B see identical length sequences.
- The benchmark really ran 16 slots: results/status-ab-fcb-T16-142fcb-short.json reports serving 16 and the engine stat says '4 groups of 4'; the server's '[strata] ... it will use 8' line is only the unpatched PARALLEL_MAX constant in server.py line 2326.
- Greedy for A/B is well reasoned for exact changes (same tokens, only speed differs), and the T16-142 baseline was re-measured under greedy at #49 rather than compared across temperatures.
- The exactness matrix (batch_test16/32, interleave, batch_overlap_test, logits dumps) is unusually thorough for the fresh-admission path, and the open slot-resume divergence is logged rather than hidden.
- TESTLOG discipline: every run has a row, raw-data pointer and verdict, which made every number in this review reproducible from results/sweep.jsonl and the engine logs.

**Points the verifiers added:**
- README summary compares Strata at 16 users with vLLM Flash-Next at 64 users without saying so: sweep rows vllm031-flashnext-pc-p100 u=16 621.8 vs u=64 710.0, p1000 u=16 385.8 vs u=24 416.0 / u=64 398.2; at matched 16 users the default (522.4/405.3) is -16 % short and +5 % long against vLLM, not the -26 % / -1 % the table (lines 12-13) and line 21-22 ('vLLM delivers more total throughput with many concurrent new prompts') imply - the user count belongs in the Flash-Next column as it already is in the 27B column.
- The interleave exactness test has never completed on this setup: every recorded run (results/rebase142/gpu/il.out and il-sc.out, overlap/m2/il-*.out, overlap/exactness-r1/summary.txt lines 15-20/168-173, fixedcost/final/summary.txt, fixedcost/s2) stops after step 3 with 'D gave way: None' and 'engine: ERR BGEN: no such free slot (--batch 3) or a bad max_new' (rc=1); TESTLOG #45/#48 report 'interleave = reference' without this caveat (only fixedcost/s2/notes.txt says 'stops at step 4 like the reference'). Steps 4-7 of batch_interleave_test.py (BYIELD give-way, solo continuation of a request left alone in a slot, client-resent history from the turn checkpoint) are exactly the multi-turn client paths.
- 'short ≈ 120 prompt tokens' (TESTLOG line 5, README line 69) is stale: every measured short row reports prompt_tok_avg 166-178 (the user message alone tokenises to 157, the chat template adds ~12), so read-time modelling for 'short' should use ~170 tokens, as the fixedcost/profile drivers already do (150-170).
- The arm label is part of the prompt (bench_concurrency.py line 48), so the short prompt length varies with the label: ab-lv-T16-142fca-s122437-short 176 tokens vs ab-fcb 169 (+7 tokens = +4 % read work on the short arm; by #51's ~0.6 ms/token slope ~4 ms x 32 admissions, ~0.4 % of a 31 s wall) - small but systematic and avoidable (fixed-width label or header outside the prompt).
- TTFT/e2e p95 in a single ab_quick arm is interpolated between the two slowest of 32 requests (bench_concurrency.py pct(), lines 36-42; requests=32 in every 16-user row), so p95 verdicts taken from single runs (#49 overlap p95 4.71 vs 3.58; #56/#57 'TTFT p95 3.51/7.32') rest on two samples each; the #41/#46 p95 statements used 3 reps (96 samples) and are the only robust ones.
- The pure-decode rate used as the calibration base (616.6 rows/s, results/profile2/dec_rows.txt) was measured sampled at t=0.7 (profile2/notes.txt 'BATCH DECODE (no admissions, sampled t=0.7)', prof_driver.py line 144 passes temperature=), while the HTTP runs it is compared with are greedy; the stall decomposition is insensitive (D=625 moves the MIX residual from -6.7 % to -6.1 %) but a greedy dec16 arm (~1 GPU minute) would make the ceiling figure consistent with the protocol.
- The MIX reference is consumed downstream: upstream/PROFILE-next-levers.md lines 285-286 and 317-334 fit the 'Mixed short / long' lever column to #53 (f = 0.76/0.54, 'effective D of about 537 at an average reply of 320 tokens'); any correction of the MIX number (tail-free mode, fcb re-run) should be propagated into that table.
- MIX TTFT p95 is set by the synchronized start, not by steady state: all 16 users start together (bench_concurrency.py lines 116-117), so round 1 queues 16 reads; ab-mix rows 388-391 show p95 3.16 s / max 3.96 s (short) and 7.12 / 8.79 s (long) = ~14-16 reads x 0.2-0.5 s, while p50 is 0.21/0.54 s. With k ~ 1 after the first burst (TESTLOG #51), 16 of 64 samples come from the start burst and p95 lands inside it, so #53's 'overlap worsens p95 (3.16->4.17, 7.12->10.13)' compares start bursts, the very lockstep situation MIX was meant to remove. Fix: staggered start or exclude round 1 from the latency percentiles (a few lines, no GPU).
- The MMQ group-sort lever is already built and engine-level measured on natural text: Strata-142 commit faddbb2e (STRATA_MMQ_GROUP_SORT=1, binary strata-g3-142lv), results/levers/mmq-sort/notes.txt: 1.2K read 428.6 -> 363.4 ms (-15 %), 150-token 145.8 -> 136.2 ms, prefill_mmq_tab_test 890/890, R dumps byte-identical, batch_test16 16/16; no TESTLOG row yet. Only the HTTP ab_quick confirmation (2 arms, ~10 GPU min) is pending, so the filler question cannot flip its verdict and the engine-level work must not be redone.
- TESTLOG line 5 and README line 69 define 'short' as ~120 prompt tokens, but no row in sweep.jsonl has prompt_tok_avg between 100 and 140: 'short' has been 162-178 tokens since strata-G16-short (162) through ab-fcb (169), consistent with --prompt-tok 100 -> one 72-token FILLER plus a ~96-token wrapper. Doc error only; comparisons across series are unaffected.
- README 'Recommended configuration' (lines 47-60) is stale for the throughput config too, not only the agent one: it names configs/T16-direct-pleram.json, engine strata-g3b and split 12,24,36 (0.1.41), while start-strata.sh line 13 starts configs/T16-142fcb.json (0.1.42 + 5 patch series, split 12,24,37). A reader following the README would deploy a config ~25 % slower than the measured default (417/297 vs 522/405).


## Lens: exactness

### [high] No token-level exactness check runs through serve/server.py; every proof is engine-level stdin (GEN/BGEN)
*key `no-production-path-token-check` · gap · status: **confirmed**, reviewer confidence 0.95*

**Claim.** All slot==solo evidence comes from tools driving `strata --serve` directly (batch_test16/32, interleave, overlap, soaks, edge drivers). The two server runs that exist (results/overlap/exactness-r1/e2e.sh, results/rebase142/http.sh) only count ok/errors, and they run bench_concurrency at its default temperature 0.7 (neither passes --temperature 0). The server-specific transitions the production path takes at every load ramp - first request on the solo path then STOP + BGEN with prompt+generated (server.py:1240-1249), a request left alone going back to the solo path (SOLO_AGAIN_MAX=2, server.py:1222), waiting=N / ckpt / pin keys - are never token-checked, and the only production-path artefact recorded today cannot detect a divergence: every 16-user ab-* row in results/sweep.jsonl has out_tok_total=16384 (32 x 512, every reply hit max_tok).

**Evidence.** bench_concurrency.py:45-103 (row has only counts, no text); bench_concurrency.py:48 prompt header `[request {uid} / {a.label} / {time.time_ns()}]` (label differs per arm: ab_quick.sh passes --label ab-$TAG-$n-short/-long); ab_quick.sh passes --temperature 0 but e2e.sh:15-19 and http.sh:19-23 do not; fc_driver.py (fixedcost/*/drv-*) uses production args but engine stdin and compares arm vs arm only; sweep.jsonl ab-* rows listed: all users=16 rows out_tok_total=16384, cached=0.

**Recommendation.** (a) Make the ab_quick prompt deterministic (drop a.label and time_ns from the header when temperature==0 - a fresh server per arm has an empty prompt cache, uid already keeps prompts unique within a run) and record sha256 of each reply's concatenated text per uid in the JSON row; a 10-line compare script prints 'tokens identical / DIFFERS at uid ...' between two arms. ~30 min of coding, zero extra GPU time; from then on every ab_quick A/B is also a production-path exactness test (512-token replies, solo<->slot migrations, group assignment, overlap variant). State per arm whether equality is expected (prefill-chunk or quant changes are not exact by design). (b) Re-run eval/quick_eval.py against the current default and diff against eval/answers-strata-ud-iq4xs.jsonl by (set,i): 390 greedy requests, 8 concurrent, up to 2048-token CoT, EOS endings, slot reuse over hundreds of admissions, through the server - ~10-15 GPU minutes (the 0.1.41 run took 14.3 min). Caveat: the stored file holds only text[-400:] (quick_eval.py:128) and comes from the pre-rebase 0.1.41 engine (TESTLOG #17), so store the full text or its sha256 this time and treat a mismatch vs the old file as 'bisect at engine level', not as a verdict. Do both: the hash is the per-A/B gate, the eval the periodic deep check.

- **Facts verifier: confirmed** [high] Checked: bench_concurrency.py:102-103 returns only counts (uid/ttft/gen/prompt/cached/err, no text); :48 header carries a.label and time_ns; :151 default temperature 0.7, and neither results/overlap/exactness-r1/e2e.sh:15-19 nor results/rebase142/http.sh:19-23 passes --temperature 0 (ab_quick.sh:11,13 does). server.py:1222 SOLO_AGAIN_MAX=2 and :1240-1249 generate_batched (solo -> STOP -> BGEN with prompt+generated) are real and never token-checked; fixedcost/final/drive.sh drives fc_driver.py over engine stdin and compares off vs on only. sweep.jsonl: 42 ab-* rows, all cached_tok=0; the number is slightly wrong: 38 of the 16-user rows have out_tok_total=16384 but the 4 ab-mix rows have 20258 (ROUNDS=4, MIX=128) - which I recomputed equals the sum of per-uid max_tok for uids 1000000..1000063, so every reply still hit its cap and no row can show a divergence. Also bench_multiturn.py:72 drops the reply text (r.pop('text')), so the agent benchmark leaves no token artefact either. On (b): the stored eval baseline was produced by engine/strata 0.1.41 with --prefill auto --layer-split auto at 12 workers (results/engine-strata-eval-iq4xs.log line 1, status-strata-eval-iq4xs.json), so a diff against it will differ for chunking reasons alone; only a fresh same-server pair is a gate. Recommendation (a) is actionable and cheap.
- **Materiality verifier: confirmed** [high] Verified: bench_concurrency.py rows carry counts only (lines 102-103, 126-135) and the prompt header `[request {uid} / {a.label} / {time.time_ns()}]` (line 48) differs per arm; ab_quick.sh passes --temperature 0 but results/overlap/exactness-r1/e2e.sh:15-19 and results/rebase142/http.sh:19-23 do not (default 0.7), and the other two server-level scripts (results/batchadmit/qprobe/run.sh, auxab/run.sh) are speed probes. Recomputed from results/sweep.jsonl: 42 ab-* rows; 38 have out_tok_total 16384 (32x512); the 4 ab-mix rows have 20258, which equals exactly the sum of the per-request caps (16 users x 4 rounds, min 128) that I recomputed - so in every row every reply hit its cap and counts cannot detect a divergence (the finding's 'every row = 16384' is slightly off, the substance holds). The production transitions are real: prompt_cache defaults to 6 (generate.cpp:723) so the INFO line reports slot_cache=1 (generate.cpp:9192) and the solo-again return (server.py:1222-1231) is live; the fcb engine log shows the ramp migration right after a 1-token solo GEN: 'prompt 170 tokens = 169 reused + 1 read' (engine-ab-fcb-T16-142fcb.log, last start, rel. lines 148/153; server.py:1316 `prompt = list(ids) + out`). Zero extra GPU time for (a) is right. Two refinements: keep a per-run tag (e.g. prompt_tok) in the header, because the short and long runs on one server share uid bases (bench_concurrency.py:165, base 1_000_000 for both); and because the migration points (solo->slot at each ramp, slot->solo when alone) are timing-dependent and not yet shown token-exact at engine level, run finding 2's migration test first or a DIFFERS between two arms is ambiguous. (b): the stored eval is engine 0.1.41 with 12 slots (status-strata-eval-iq4xs.json), 14.3 min (eval_results.jsonl), text[-400:] only (quick_eval.py:128) - a periodic deep check worth 10-15 GPU min, not a per-A/B gate.

### [medium] batch_interleave_test stops at step 4 in every arm here: slot->solo continuation and turn-checkpoint resume are untested on this machine
*key `interleave-steps-4-7-never-run` · gap · status: **confirmed**, reviewer confidence 0.9*

**Claim.** On a layer split the engine pipelines the read and 'a pipelined read never gives way' (generate.cpp:171, :10000), so step 4's BYIELD is dropped, D keeps decoding in slot 2, the test re-sends D into the now-active slot and gets 'ERR BGEN: no such free slot' (generate.cpp:10087-10091) - rc 1 in il-ref, il-off, il-on, il_ref_min, il_on (reference engine too). Steps 6 (a request stopped in its slot and continued on the solo path - exactly what the server does for a request left alone) and 7 (F from the turn checkpoint without the reply's thinking) therefore never execute. TESTLOG #37/#40/#45/#48 and the lever notes cite 'interleave identical / = reference', which is true for steps 1-3 only.

**Evidence.** batch_interleave_test.py:142-176 (step 4), :177-196 (step 6), :211-224 (step 7); results/levers/self-commit/il_ref.out and il_on.out end with 'D gave way: None' + 'engine: ERR BGEN: no such free slot (--batch 3)'; exactness-r1/summary.txt il-off/il-ref rc=1 the same way; rebase142/gpu/notes.txt 'il: rc 1 ... at step 4 (pre-existing)'; engine log 'strata generate: --batch-groups auto: 3 groups of 1 slot' (il_ref.engine.log).

**Recommendation.** Patch the test (tools are ours to change): skip step 4 when the engine is pipelined (or add --no-yield), so steps 5-7 run; alternatively run a second arm with --batch-groups 1 where BYIELD is honoured. Then add an explicit migration test reusing batch_test16.Engine: GEN A, STOP after k tokens, BGEN slot0 with A+tokens (server.py's solo->slot move), decode to the end == solo A; and the reverse (step 6). ~1 h of work, ~3 GPU minutes per run on the fcb config with the production env.

- **Facts verifier: confirmed** [medium] All 19 il*.out files under results/ (fixedcost ab/final/k1k2/s1/s2/step0-o1, levers/self-commit il_on/il_ref/il_ref_min/il_sc, exactness-r1 il-off/-on/-ref/-off-1325, m2, rebase142 il/il-sc) end with 'D gave way: None' + 'engine: ERR BGEN: no such free slot (--batch 3)'; the engine log says '--batch-groups auto: 3 groups of 1 slot' / 'batch (pipelined, 3 groups of 1)' and fixedcost/s2/il-on.engine.log:875-876 traces 'drain for BYIELD: 0 groups in flight' / 'BYIELD dropped, 0 groups in flight'. generate.cpp:170-172 and :9999-10000 state the drop, :10090-10091 the ERR (bs[b].active). batch_interleave_test.py:142-176 (step 4), :177-196 (step 6), :211-224 (step 7) as cited; the SystemExit at the ERR skips 5-7. origin/main has not touched the test (2 commits, same on both branches). Minor: TESTLOG #45 does not mention interleave (#37/#40/#48 and README:257 do).
- **Materiality verifier: confirmed** [medium] All 20 il*.out files under results/ (exactness-r1, m2, rebase142/gpu, fixedcost/{ab,k1k2,s1,s2,step0-o1,final}, levers/self-commit incl. il_ref_min) end with 'D gave way: None' + 'engine: ERR BGEN: no such free slot (--batch 3)'; none contains the step-6/7 output lines. Cause verified: generate.cpp:170-172 and :10000 ('a pipelined read never gives way'), ERR at :10090 because slot 2 is still active; engine log '--batch-groups auto: 3 groups of 1 slot'. TESTLOG #37/#40/#45/#48 say 'interleave identical / = reference' - true for steps 1-3 only. Upstream origin/main has not changed tools/batch_interleave_test.py (git diff g3-142..origin/main: 0 lines), so the fix is ours to make. Materiality is real: slot_cache=1 by default means the slot->solo return (step 6's path) runs in production whenever a request is left alone, and the solo->slot move happens at every ramp (engine log '169 reused + 1 read'); neither direction has an engine-level token check here. ~3 GPU minutes, changes the per-default gate.

### [medium] Batch-vs-solo equality under sampling with a fixed seed was never run (batch_test16 --keys unused); only arm-vs-arm seeded runs exist
*key `sampled-batch-vs-solo-untested` · gap · status: **confirmed**, reviewer confidence 0.9*

**Claim.** batch_test16 accepts --keys but no run.sh, TESTLOG row or results/*.out uses it. The seeded-sampled drivers (ab_edge.py KEYS incl. penalties, ckpt_edge/s1_edge/fix_edge 'temperature=0.7 seed=11', fc_driver 'temperature=0.7 seed=uid') compare switch-off vs switch-on with both arms in slots, so they prove the switches do not change the sampled path, not that a sampled slot equals its sampled solo run (BATCHING.md's Philox(seed, position) claim, verify.cpp:2187-2188, :2893). Production users run at temperature 0.7. Note also that penalties are by design not applied in batch windows (BATCHING.md 'Limits'), so a request with repetition/presence penalty is slot != solo regardless; the server forwards penalties when a client sends them (server.py:970-988).

**Evidence.** grep -rn -E '--keys|temperature=' over linux/TESTLOG.md, README.md, results/**/run.sh, *.out: hits only in fc_driver.py:150, ab_edge.py:17-18, ckpt_edge.py:22, s1_edge.py:25, fix_edge.py:28 (all BGEN-vs-BGEN); upstream/ANALYSIS-batching-levers.md:354,371 planned 'batch_test.py --keys temperature=0.7' and it was not done; the engine takes seed only when given (generate.cpp:10406, :11632), so the keys must include seed=N.

**Recommendation.** One run: batch_test16.py --batch 16 --n 16 --max-new 128 --keys "temperature=0.7 top_k=20 seed=7" with the fcb config, production env and exact flags, dump + a repeat with --long-tokens 1200 (~2 GPU minutes each). If it passes, add it to the per-default gate; if it fails, that is an upstream design limit to document, not a switch defect (confirm by running the reference engine the same way).

### [medium] The 'next turn from the parent's slot differs from a fresh solo read' row is a wrong reference, not an engine defect
*key `slot-resumed-turn-reference-mismatch` · inconsistency · status: **weakened**, reviewer confidence 0.8*

**Claim.** soak64b/reuse4 compare a child turn that RESUMEd ~845 tokens of decode-path state from its slot against a solo GEN issued after other GENs, i.e. a fresh PREFILL re-read of parent prompt + reply (the live session no longer holds the parent; no prompt cache in T16-mmqtab). batch_interleave_test's step 3 instead issues the solo next turn right after the parent's solo GEN, so its reference continues from the live session (BATCHING.md:115-116) - and that comparison is IDENTICAL in every arm. The data fit 'resume vs re-read' exactly: children that happened to get RESUME 0 in a slot (fresh read) are IDENTICAL to the fresh solo (soak2-on2 r16/r32/r48, soak2-off r16/r40/r48), children with RESUME 803-847 differ at token 15-54 for 4 of 6 prompts and are the same tokens in the reference engine, overlap off and overlap on (reuse-ref/-off/-on), and r24/r56 stay identical even when resumed - the prompt-dependent near-tie pattern of two numerically different but valid paths (chunked MMQ prefill writing int8 KV vs per-token decode GEMV + GDN state). By transitivity (slot-resumed == live-continued solo; slot-resumed != fresh-read solo) the same divergence exists inside the solo path, where upstream also continues live conversations.

**Evidence.** soak64b.py:57-61 (child solo computed in loop order, after unrelated GENs), reuse4.py:31-36 (S[c] from soak2-on.json), batch_interleave_test.py:79-81 vs :128-141; exactness-r1/soak2-*.out child lines (RESUME 0 -> IDENTICAL; RESUME 846/847/845/803 -> DIFFERS at 54/18/15/30), reuse-ref.out == reuse-on.out token for token; TESTLOG.md:59 row 'open, upstream behaviour?'; notes.txt:13-15.

**Recommendation.** Close the row with a 2-minute solo-only confirmation: GEN parent; GEN child (live-continued) vs GEN unrelated; GEN child (fresh read) on one engine - if they differ, the divergence is the solo path's own. Then fix the reference in soak64b/reuse4 (compute the child's solo right after the parent's solo, as the interleave test does) and add one sentence to BATCHING.md 'Exactness': the reference for a continued turn is the solo run continued from the same state, not a fresh read. ~1 h, ~3 GPU minutes.

- **Facts verifier: confirmed** [medium] Stronger than stated. soak64b.py:54-61 computes each child's solo in loop order (parent at i-7, 6 unrelated GENs in between); the engine's default --prompt-cache keeps 6 checkpoints (generate.cpp:873), so the parent's is evicted: soak2-on.engine.log shows every child solo as a fresh read - 'prompt 837/868/866/869/867/825/868 tokens = 0 reused' (lines 910, 1623, 2323, 3037, 3733, 4434, 5142). The interleave test's A2 solo instead continues the live chain ('244 tokens = 225 reused + 19 read', levers/self-commit/il_ref.engine.log:129) and its slot run (224 reused, :187) is IDENTICAL in every arm. soak2-*.out: RESUME 0 children identical (on2 r16/r32/r48, off/ref r16/r40/r48), RESUME 803-847 children differ at 54/18/15/30 for r16/r32/r40/r48 while r24/r56 stay identical when resumed; reuse-ref/-on/-off.out differ only in the first line (engine/env), lines 2-8 byte-identical. TESTLOG.md:59 row and exactness-r1/notes.txt:13-15 as cited. The 'same divergence inside the solo path' is a hypothesis the 2-minute recommendation tests.
- **Materiality verifier: weakened** [medium] Data verified: soak64b.py:54-61 computes a child's solo in loop order after the unrelated solos (the live session then holds another conversation), soak2-off/-ref/-ref2/-on2 reuse soak2-on's solo via --solo-json; child lines: every RESUME-0 child is IDENTICAL (r08 x5, r16 in on2/off/ref/ref2, r32 in on2, r40 in on/off/ref/ref2, r48 in on2/off/ref), every DIFFERS has RESUME 803-847 (r16@54, r32@18, r40@15, r48@30), r24/r56 stay IDENTICAL though resumed, and reuse-ref/-off/-on produce the same tokens on three engines; interleave step 3 is IDENTICAL in all 20 arms. The 'resume vs fresh read' hypothesis fits all 24 child rows. But the transitivity link 'slot-resumed == live-continued solo' rests on one prompt (A2, 169 tokens) and r24/r56 show a single resumed prompt can be identical by luck; the alternative 'upstream RESUME-state artefact present in the reference engine too' is not excluded by existing data. The recommended 2-minute solo-only experiment is exactly what decides it and is proportionate; it changes what gets reported upstream and closes TESTLOG line 59 ('open, upstream behaviour?').

Keep the TESTLOG row open until the solo-only check (GEN parent; GEN child live-continued vs GEN child after an unrelated GEN) has run.
