### [low] Unit tests are solid; concurrent guard zeroing has no unit test but is structurally safe and covered by every ov-on arm
*key `unit-tests-and-guard-concurrency` · confirmation · status: **confirmed**, reviewer confidence 0.85*

**Claim.** prefill_mmq_tab_test (890/890, with the 'guards not zeroed -> non-finite output, as required' negative control and the 16 group-order cases), gdn_parity (multi-T and per-slot kernels bitwise against the single-token / per-row kernels, 0 failures) and expert_cache_slot_guard_test (stride/offset/room/tail on uniform and sized caches, guard zeroable, default layout unchanged) cover the kernels and layout. The guard is zeroed on the prompt stream at the group end (prefill.cpp:3783-3787) and lies past each blob, decode reads only blob bytes, nothing writes cache slots during a run (0 swaps; the overlap gate requires 100% residency), so an overlapped read racing decode on the same card cannot change decode inputs; every ov-on arm runs with STRATA_MMQ_DIRECT=1 and 22 overlapped reads beside 14 slots and is token-identical.

**Evidence.** results/levers/mmq-sort/prefill_mmq_tab_test.txt tail; results/levers/self-commit/gdn_parity.txt; tests/core/expert_cache_slot_guard_test.cpp:34-109; CMakeLists.txt:570-573, :863-866, :1456-1459; exactness-r1/run.sh:15 (STRATA_MMQ_DIRECT=1 on all arms), ov-on.out 'log: 22 overlapped reads'.

**Recommendation.** No unit test needed now. If a non-resident configuration (adaptive swaps > 0) ever enables the overlap, add a test that a swap into a slot during an overlapped read does not race the guard zeroing - today that case is excluded by the gate.

- **Facts verifier: confirmed** [low] results/levers/mmq-sort/prefill_mmq_tab_test.txt: '890 comparisons, 0 failed', negative controls at lines 6/9/18 'guards not zeroed: non-finite output, as required', 'group order: 16 comparisons, all bitwise identical'; levers/self-commit/gdn_parity.txt: multi-T and per-slot kernels bitwise, 'gdn: 0 failures'; tests/core/expert_cache_slot_guard_test.cpp exists (117 lines, stride/guard checks at :34-39); CMakeLists.txt:570-573, :863-866, :1456-1459 register the three tests; prefill.cpp:3785 'read in its slot: the guard after the blob is zeroed at the group end'; exactness-r1/run.sh:15 sets STRATA_MMQ_DIRECT=1 on every arm; ov-on arms log 22 overlapped reads. One nuance: 'nothing writes cache slots during a run' holds for the T16 configs (0 exchanged) but not for a borrowing config such as T32-142 (the prompt path borrows and refills slots, generate.cpp:643-644); the overlap gate excludes lending (README:254), so the conclusion stands.
- **Materiality verifier: confirmed** [low] levers/mmq-sort/prefill_mmq_tab_test.txt: three 'negative control (guards not zeroed): non-finite output, as required' lines and '890 comparisons, 0 failed'; levers/self-commit/gdn_parity.txt: multi-T and per-slot kernels bitwise against single-token/per-row, 'gdn: 0 failures'; tests/core/expert_cache_slot_guard_test.cpp exists (slot_stride/guard cases), CMakeLists.txt:570-573, 863-866, 1456-1459 register the three tests; prefill.cpp:3785 'read in its slot: the guard after the blob is zeroed at the group end'. The structural argument also holds for the drained read: batch_read_piece() returns 0 when piped (generate.cpp:22-27), so no slot decodes beside a non-overlapped read, and the overlap gate refuses when anything is lent (README:254) - consistent with the T32 loan being outside the overlap's reach.

**Done well (this lens):**
- The exactness matrix for the overlap (env on/off/reference, two splits, forced pad-up/wait, 64-request soak, 5 fault arms) and the per-step fixed-cost edge drivers (greedy / seeded / penalties, root and turn checkpoints, kv-resident 21K prompt) are extensive and every arm's command line is preserved in summary.txt.
- Every batch_test16/32 and batch_overlap_test dump across 9 engine builds, 3 splits and all switch sets hashes to the same solo and batch token sets (recomputed here) - the cross-engine invariance the TESTLOG claims is real.
- Lever 3 was gated by a bitwise R-dump of 2965 x 10240 BF16 activation rows plus a negative-control probe, which is stronger than any token comparison and sampling-independent.
- The exact-flag conditions are genuinely inert for T16/T24/T32 (100% resident, no loans, 0 swaps, 0 file reads), so the engine-level tests compute what production computes.
- prefill_mmq_tab_test includes a negative control for the slot guard and the new group-order cases; gdn_parity checks the multi-T and per-slot variants bitwise.
- Test prompts include a 1.2K prompt read beside decoding slots in every batch_test16 run (--long-tokens 1200) and a 5K prompt with a root checkpoint in every overlap run.

**Points the verifiers added:**
- T32-142 production borrows cache slots: engine-strata-T32-142-{short,long}.log (last engine start, lines +75-76) 'CUDA3 prompt path borrows 166 of its 6144 slots (0.39 GiB)' / 'lend sizing: ... 166 of them keep their experts in RAM too', every request '0.39 GiB of experts in RAM'; BATCHING.md:197-199 says those experts run on the CPU for the decoding slots during a read, i.e. batch != solo rounding is possible; every bt32 proof (TESTLOG #45, README:122-123) ran with --no-prefill-borrow. Fix: add --no-prefill-borrow to configs/T32-142.json (or split 12,24,37) and confirm 4x 'no loan' in the log before claiming 32-slot exactness.
- The quick_eval baseline cannot serve as an exactness reference: eval/answers-strata-ud-iq4xs.jsonl was produced by engine/strata (setup build, status engine 0.1.41) with --prefill auto --layer-split auto at 12 workers (results/engine-strata-eval-iq4xs.log line 1), while the default uses --prefill 512 and 12,24,37; chunking changes MMQ grouping (mmq-sort/cmp.txt: group order changes every row), so a text diff will differ regardless of batching. The usable production-path gate is a fresh pair on the same server (quick_eval --workers 1 vs 8, full text or sha256), ~2 x 14 min.
- bench_multiturn.py:60,72 collects the reply text and then drops it (r.pop('text')); results/multiturn.jsonl holds only timings. The same per-uid sha256 proposed for ab_quick would, in bench_multiturn, token-check the agent path (conversation-cache resume across 5 turns, the path finding 3 calls uncovered) for free in every multi-turn run.
- --prompt-cache-every 8192 (mid-prompt checkpoints at chunk boundaries, set in all four configs/X16-agent-*.json) has no exactness run: no hit in results/**/{*.sh,*.out,*.py}, TESTLOG.md or README.md; ckpt_edge.py covers root and turn checkpoints only.
- The soak2 reference mismatch has a concrete cause that also tells how to fix the driver: the engine's default --prompt-cache keeps 6 checkpoints (generate.cpp:873) and soak64b.py:54-61 puts exactly 6 unrelated GENs between a parent (i-7) and its child (i), so every child solo was a fresh read ('= 0 reused', soak2-on.engine.log lines 910, 1623, 2323, 3037, 3733, 4434, 5142) while the interleave test's A2 solo reused 225 tokens (il_ref.engine.log:129). Either compute the child's solo right after the parent's or start the test engine with --prompt-cache >= 8.
- The production solo<->slot migrations are not just untested, they are frequent and the hash gate of finding 1 depends on them: at every 16-user ramp the first request runs solo, is STOPped and re-sent as BGEN with prompt+out (server.py:1316); the fcb engine log shows it ('1 generated in 13 ms' then 'prompt 170 tokens = 169 reused + 1 read', engine-ab-fcb-T16-142fcb.log last start rel. lines 148/153), and the slot->solo return is live because prompt_cache defaults to 6 (generate.cpp:723 -> slot_cache=1 at :9192, server.py:1230). Migration points vary with timing between arms, so an arm-vs-arm text hash can only be interpreted once GEN->STOP->BGEN(prompt+out) and BGEN->BSTOP->GEN(prompt+out) are shown token-exact at engine level (finding 2's migration test, ~3 GPU min) - sequence that test before adopting the hash gate, or compare only never-migrated requests.
- Single-user long-prompt speed with the new default looks lower, contradicting TESTLOG #57 / README:315 'single user unchanged': sweep.jsonl ab-fcb 1-user long rows show dec_tps_p50 121.8 (T16-142fcb) vs 131.7 (T16-142fca) and agg 108.7 vs 115.3 tok/s, and the engine logs give drafts accepted 276/488 + 281/450 (fcb) vs 283/444 + 315/439 (fca) for those 1234-token requests; the short rows (130.9 vs 129.6) are the only ones TESTLOG cites. n=2 per arm, so not conclusive, but MULTI_GPU.md says STRATA_SPLIT_MTP_BATCH can move draft acceptance (upstream saw about -3% decode). A 5-minute 1-user A/B (LEVELS=1 ROUNDS=8, or the parity tool's 'drafts accepted' readout) would settle whether the default costs single-user speed on long prompts.
- README.md's 'Recommended configuration' section is stale and sends agent users to the untested config: README.md:49 recommends configs/T16-direct-pleram.json and configs/X16-agent-direct.json, README.md:59 names engine strata-g3b (0.1.41), while start-strata.sh:12 and README.md:315 use configs/T16-142fcb.json. A 5-minute doc fix that also bounds finding 3 (say which configs the exactness claims cover).
- server.py prints a wrong slot count at every production start: '"parallel": 16 - the engine runs at most 8 requests together; it will use 8' (Strata-142/serve/server.py:2308 PARALLEL_MAX=8, :2326-2328; seen twice in results/server-ab-fcb-T16-142fcb-short.out) although the patched engine runs 16 (status-ab-fcb-T16-142fcb-short.json serving 16; engine log '--batch-groups auto: 4 groups of 4 slots'). Cosmetic, but patch 0001 lifted the limit and patch 0020 already touches server.py, so the PR should carry the one-line fix.

### [medium] Pointer-table MMQ: the opt-in form matches upstream's precedent, but a 552-line mirror of ggml's scheduler plus 36-38 MiB VRAM per GPU under EAGER loading needs a design issue before a 1,900-line PR
*key `mmq-direct-acceptance-risk` · risk · status: **weakened**, reviewer confidence 0.7*

**Claim.** The packaging is upstream-grade: an OFF CMake option exactly like STRATA_MMQ_KQUANTS/STRATA_ORCA_Q4KS_MMQ/STRATA_Q6K_EXPERTS, env opt-in like STRATA_SM70_TABLE ('opt-in until confirmed'), test registered with SKIP_RETURN_CODE 77, and the build without it provably byte-identical. The acceptance risk is the mechanism: moe_mmq_tab.cuh mirrors mmq.cuh's scheduler and is pinned by a SHA-256 fingerprint that silently turns the feature off (STATUS message only) on every ggml bump, so the maintainers inherit a re-verification duty; the VRAM cost under EAGER is the other objection, and 0009 notes it vanishes under LAZY.

**Evidence.** CMakeLists.txt (g3-142) 85-93 option text and 1382-1421 fingerprint block (known hash 36e4dc71..., message 'STRATA_MMQ_TAB off: mmq.cuh differs'); src/prefill/moe_mmq_tab.cuh 552 lines; tests/cuda/prefill_mmq_tab_test.cu 625 lines; upstream precedent CMakeLists v0.1.42:76-84 (three OFF MMQ options), docs/NVIDIA_V100.md:85-89 and fused_gr.cu:1822 (STRATA_SM70_TABLE opt-in until confirmed), native_mmvq.cu:2601/2655 (STRATA_MMVQ_IL_Q3K default on, =0 off). Effect: TESTLOG #37 read -18..-24 %, +6 %/+10 % at 16 users; 874/874 and (GROUP_SORT) 890/890 bitwise. ddd995e1: '36-38 MiB of VRAM per GPU (sm_86 ...; equal under LAZY)'. Group E size: 1,735+/100- (+207 for faddbb2e).

**Recommendation.** Open a feature-request issue first ('What do you want to do?': read resident experts in place; the gather is ~31 % of a short prompt's GPU time; numbers; the three design options from ISSUE.md Finding 4) and ask which form they would merge: (a) the mirrored scheduler behind the OFF option, (b) a pointer-table variant in their ggml fork, or (c) nothing. Offer to load the tab TUs lazily (per-module CUDA_MODULE_LOADING or cuModuleLoad on first use) to remove the VRAM objection - measure once (~20 min). Only after the answer prepare the PR (3-4 h incl. squash). Add sm_80/sm_89/sm_120 to docs/TEST_REQUESTS.md since whole_tiles()/overread_bytes() are per-arch (moe_mmq.cu:319-372).

### [medium] The --aux-cpus thread-inheritance finding is an upstream bug report that needs no patch and is still unaddressed on origin/main
*key `aux-cpus-upstream-report` · suggestion · status: **weakened**, reviewer confidence 0.85*

**Claim.** TESTLOG #52 shows the engine's pinned host thread passes CPU 0 to every thread it creates (19 threads on one core; host grouping 5 -> 60-68 ms per card per chunk) unless --aux-cpus is given. Upstream has not touched this since v0.1.42. It fits the bug_report form exactly and the series already contains the fix pattern (widen the mask before placing a worker).

**Evidence.** linux/TESTLOG.md row 52; linux/README.md:302; `git log v0.1.42..origin/main | grep -i 'aux|affin'` empty and no `aux_cpus`/`sched_setaffinity` lines in the upstream generate.cpp diff; our 0025 does the widening for the checkpoint threads (generate.cpp diff lines 215-221: `cpu_set_t all; ... sched_setaffinity(0, sizeof all, &all)`), and c9bbd1fc's first fix is about that mask.

**Recommendation.** File a bug report (What happened: the 19-threads-on-CPU-0 measurement with STRATA_PREFILL_TIMING=1 lines; Strata version/GPU/OS; engine log) and propose either that worker threads be created with the process mask or that `--aux-cpus auto` be the default on a layer split; attach the aux arms from TESTLOG #52. ~30 min, no GPU.

- **Facts verifier: confirmed** [medium] TESTLOG row 52 verified (19 threads on one core, host grouping 5 -> 60-68 ms, 1.2K read 557 -> 484 ms, 65-127 492.9/376.2 vs 493.0/357.0). linux/README.md:300-304 verified. `git log v0.1.42..origin/main | grep -iE 'aux|affin'` has no relevant commit and the upstream generate.cpp diff adds no aux_cpus/sched_setaffinity line. The widening code exists only on g3-142 (generate.cpp:1648 `sched_setaffinity(0, sizeof all, &all)`; 0 hits in v0.1.42), but it sits in patch 0025 lines 159-162 and 0028 lines 38-41, not 'diff lines 215-221'; in c9bbd1fc it is the second paragraph (STRATA_CKPT_THREADS), not the first. docs/TEST_REQUESTS.md even lists 'Three or four NVIDIA GPUs | No multi-GPU NVIDIA rig of that size here' as wanted, so the report fits an open request.
- **Materiality verifier: weakened** [medium] TESTLOG #52 and the README 'CPU placement of the prefill threads' section confirmed; `git log v0.1.42..origin/main | grep -iE 'aux|affin'` is empty and the upstream generate.cpp diff only adds `#include "strata/platform/aux_cpus.hpp"`; b7350501 widens the mask (`cpu_set_t all; ... sched_setaffinity(0, sizeof all, &all)` under __linux__) and c9bbd1fc's second fix is about that mask. But the inheritance is documented upstream behaviour, not an unknown defect: v0.1.42 docs/DETAILS.md:1672-1674 'A thread the engine starts after that inherits the host's one CPU: the adaptive tier's job thread, the prefill helpers, the router look-ahead', and --aux-cpus is the designed remedy (':1681 `--aux-cpus off` is the default'). So the right form is a feature request / default-change proposal under AGENTS.md:32 with #52's numbers (1.2K read 557->484 ms, 16 users long 357->376 tok/s, +5.4 %), adding the layer split's prefill stage threads to that documented list - not a bug report. Still ~30 min, no GPU, and the one upstream item that helps every Linux multi-GPU user.
  - Corrected claim: Upstream documents that spawned threads inherit the host's CPU and ships --aux-cpus (off by default) as the remedy; propose `--aux-cpus auto` by default on a layer split (or unpinned stage threads) as a measured default change via the feature-request form, with TESTLOG #52's numbers.

**Done well (this lens):**
- Opt-in discipline is real: 27 of 29 commits state the default is off/unchanged, and ddd995e1 proves it at the strongest level upstream could ask for (58 cubins + PTX byte-identical to the reference build).
- The build-flag/env-flag dependency is guarded correctly: direct_mode() warns once and returns 0, the slot guard becomes 0 and the cache layout stays byte-identical (moe_mmq.cu:374-387, generate.cpp:2878-2880).
- Exactness tooling exceeds upstream's bar: batch_test16/32, batch_overlap_test (14 slots decoding while prompts admit, fault injection), batch_interleave_test, prefill_mmq_tab_test 874/874 + 890/890 bitwise, ThreadSanitizer on the PLE reader, compute-sanitizer memcheck clean.
- Rebase hygiene: a linear series on v0.1.42, the 28 patch files byte-identical to the commits, and only 3 one-hunk conflicts against 72 upstream commits.
- The STRATA_MMQ_TAB CMake option mirrors upstream's own OFF-option pattern (STRATA_MMQ_KQUANTS, STRATA_ORCA_Q4KS_MMQ, STRATA_Q6K_EXPERTS) including test registration with SKIP_RETURN_CODE 77 and a configure-time fingerprint instead of a compile probe.
- Portability guards are in place where they matter: kPipeOverlapBuild (Linux CUDA only), !__HIPCC__ around every tab path, __linux__ around sched_setaffinity, SYCL stubs; 0019 shows the HIP build was at least reasoned about.
- Licensing handled: moe_mmq_tab.cuh carries ggml's MIT notice (0021) and THIRD_PARTY_NOTICES.md exists at the repo root.
- 0002 (direct slot copy) and 0001+0018 (>8 slots) are close to upstream-ready as written: small, measured (65.6->34.0 ms; 603 vs 345 tok/s batch decode), with the host path kept as the default.
- Commit messages generally carry the measurement, the machine, the test names and the counts (e.g. f5093737, e3c39eeb, 01a72a5f) - exactly what AGENTS.md:30-32 asks for.

**Points the verifiers added:**
- The 30th commit 7e7e169e (STRATA_BATCH_SELF_COMMIT, 03:49) is the lens's 'dirty tree' and it is a measured +5 % decode lever: its message reports 653.7 -> 686.0 rows/s (16 slots) and 714.1 -> 752.6 (32); results/levers/self-commit/*/events.log give 565.0 -> 593.9 and 536.6 -> 566.9 rows/s 'admissions included' (d16_ref/d16_sc, d32_ref/d32_sc, 03:40-03:45), exactness bt16_sc 16/16, bt32_sc 32/32, ov_sc RESULT: PASS. Together with faddbb2e (GROUP_SORT, read 428.6 -> 363.4 ms at 1.2K) these are two un-logged levers that could move the default; both need a TESTLOG row and one ab_quick pair each (the only 16-user evidence for either is missing) before T16-142fcb.json changes.
- The commit message of 7e7e169e and the raw logs use different metrics for the same runs (653.7/686.0 vs 565.0/593.9 rows/s: a 30 s decode-only window vs the engine's 'admissions included' total). The TESTLOG row should name the metric, or the next reader will think the numbers disagree. Also results/levers/self-commit/run8.log ends with a bash syntax error ('Syntaxfehler ... else', run.sh edited 03:40:57 while running) and d16_sc.driver.out is 0 bytes, so the second-round d16_sc/d32_sc figures exist only in events.log.
- All evidence for the two new levers is untracked: `git -C /media/raid0/StrataQ38FN status` shows `?? linux/results/levers/` (notes.txt, run.sh, cmp.txt, prefill_mmq_tab_test.txt, events.log) plus eight .nsys-rep files. Per the project's own rule (every experiment logged, committed, pushed) these should be added now (excluding the 60-120 MB .r64/.rall dumps and .nsys-rep), otherwise the next session cannot find that GROUP_SORT and SELF_COMMIT were already measured.
- ISSUE.md:41 ('the D gave way step fails on a split with ERR BGEN: no such free slot (--batch 3)') is still reproducible today on the reference engine (results/levers/self-commit/il_ref.out, il_ref_min.out, rc=1) and 7e7e169e's message confirms it; the lens lists it as fixed upstream. It is the concrete reproducer for the 'pipelined read never gives way' bug report and should be filed with the --aux-cpus report, not dropped.
- docs/TEST_REQUESTS.md row 'Three or four NVIDIA GPUs | No multi-GPU NVIDIA rig of that size here | #880' is an open upstream request that this 4x RTX 3090 work answers directly; a COMMUNITY_BENCHMARKS.md-style report (same-day reference run, what was not tested) would be accepted on its own and gives every later PR a measured context to cite.
- Several line references in the lens are off and would mislead an editor: PR.md 'legacy stream' is line 14 not 10; the affinity hunk is patch 0025 lines 159-162 (and 0028:38-41), not 215-221; BATCHING.md's 'decode between the prompt's chunks' is line 128; STRATA_SPLIT_MTP_BATCH is at generate.cpp 8357-8364 on g3-142, not 7957; the STRATA_MMVQ_IL_Q3K precedent does not exist in the tree.
- Rejected features are still in the series: 0017 (65aa2993, STRATA_PIPE_OVERLAP_MAX_QUEUE) and 0020 (d72209a9, server waiting=N) were judged ❌ in TESTLOG #43/#44, yet they sit in the 28-patch export and in the proposed PR-F; d72209a9 is the only commit touching serve/ (server.py 5 lines) where upstream changed +134/-4 - drop them or keep them local.
- Upstream explicitly asks for this rig: origin/main docs/TEST_REQUESTS.md row 'Three or four NVIDIA GPUs | No multi-GPU NVIDIA rig of that size here | #880'; AGENTS.md:33-35 and COMMUNITY_BENCHMARKS.md define the report (built-in tools/strata_bench.py / bench.sh, three runs per prompt size, same-day comparison, 'what you did not test'). No report or draft exists in linux/ (grep COMMUNITY_BENCHMARKS over README.md and upstream/: none). It is the highest-value zero-code upstream item and the right home for the 4x3090 numbers; GPU cost is the bench tool's three runs per size on a stock build, schedulable when the GPUs are free.
- Upstream already has a >8-slot path: v0.1.42 generate.cpp:4240 caps --batch at kVerifyMaxT only when `!batch_mtp`, and BATCHING.md:35/87 describe >8 slots rotating through eight-row windows with --batch-mtp; 650001fb's message notes '--batch-mtp keeps upstream's kVerifyMaxT rows'. PR-A's text, docs line and exactness statement must distinguish the two mechanisms (pipelined groups vs --batch-mtp rotation); no finding mentions this.
- STRATA_SLOT_CKPT_MOVE has a documented behavioural side effect (d42a987c: 'A session SAVE or a parked conversation right after an admission lacks the moved turn checkpoint (the slot holds it)'), so it cannot be part of a 'measured default-on' proposal without a note; of the two options the squash finding offers, only the umbrella opt-in switch is compatible with AGENTS.md:30 for this switch.
- Lever 3 (GROUP_SORT, faddbb2e) is arch-conditional: whole_tiles() (moe_mmq.cu, +33 lines) requires one block per tile on every visible GPU and the message says the switch does nothing at 108 or 128 SMs or on AMD's stream-k grid - so the -9.6 / -65.2 ms read gains (results/levers/mmq-sort/notes.txt) are specific to the 82-SM RTX 3090 class; any upstream line must say so, and the 16-user ab_quick before adopting it is still outstanding.
- bench_concurrency.py's headline tok/s uses the server-reported usage.completion_tokens (line 97, fallback = streamed chunk count), not a client count; in the 72 upstream commits only cache_n changed (server.py diff line 280), so the post-rebase comparison is valid, but the re-baseline should log completion_tokens against the chunk count once so a future usage-accounting change cannot masquerade as a speed change.


## Lens: docs

### [high] Report progress table mixes two protocols between 0.9.0 and 0.10.0; the fixed-cost step is overstated
*key `progress-table-protocol-break` · inconsistency · status: **confirmed**, reviewer confidence 0.95*

**Claim.** Verdict: partly supported. Every number in the table traces to the logs (Start ~88 = TESTLOG #1; ~417/~297 = #31 / CHANGELOG 0.5.0; 444/327 = #38; 467/342 = #46; 493/357 = #49; 493/376 = #52; 522/405 = #57). But 467/342 is a 3-repetition mean at temperature 0.7 (series_142.sh calls run_strata_variant.sh without --temperature, bench default 0.7), while 493/357 and later are greedy ab_quick runs. The same config (T16-142) measured both ways gives 466.9/342.1 (0.7) vs 469.7/349.4 (greedy): +0.6 % short, +2.1 % long from the protocol alone. The table's 0.9.0->0.10.0 step reads as +5.6 %/+4.4 % while the same-protocol fixed-cost gain (#49) is +4.9 %/+2.2 %; half of the long-prompt step is the protocol change.

**Evidence.** linux/TESTLOG.md #46 (T16-142 3 reps: 469.9/468.3/462.5 short, 342.5/343.2/340.5 long; sweep.jsonl labels strata-T16-142-short-r1..r3 / -long-r1..r3, users 16); linux/TESTLOG.md #49 (ab-fc.out 473.2/351.6 + ab-fc2.out 466.2/347.2 -> 469.7/349.4; fc 492.8/356.6 + 493.1/357.3 -> 492.95/356.95); linux/series_142.sh lines 7-8 (no --temperature); linux/bench_concurrency.py:151 (default 0.7); linux/ab_quick.sh:11-13 (--temperature 0); CHANGELOG.md:63 notes the protocol switch but 0.10.0 (line 53) does not say its baseline 470/349 was re-measured greedy.

**Recommendation.** In the report and CHANGELOG 0.10.0 add one clause: '(baseline re-measured greedy: 470/349; the 0.9.0 figures 467/342 were at temperature 0.7, 3 reps)'. In TESTLOG header state: '#1-#47 temperature 0.7 via run_strata_variant.sh; from #49 greedy via ab_quick.sh'. 15 minutes, no GPU.

- **Facts verifier: confirmed** [medium] Recomputed from linux/results/sweep.jsonl: strata-T16-142-short-r1..r3 @16 = 469.9/468.3/462.5 (mean 466.9), -long = 342.5/343.2/340.5 (342.1); ab-fc/ab-fc2 T16-142 = 473.2/466.2 (469.7) and 351.6/347.2 (349.4); T16-142fc = 492.8/493.1, 356.6/357.3. linux/series_142.sh:7-10 passes no --temperature (bench_concurrency.py:151 default 0.7); ab_quick.sh:11,13 pass --temperature 0. CHANGELOG.md:61 publishes 467/342 for 0.9.0 and :53 says 0.10.0 'was 470/349' with no note that the baseline was re-measured greedy; :63 only announces the protocol switch. Ratios: 493/467=+5.6 %, 357/342=+4.4 % vs same-protocol 492.95/469.7=+5.0 %, 356.95/349.4=+2.2 %. Caveat: the +0.6 %/+2.1 % cannot be attributed to temperature alone - the two greedy T16-142 starts 8 min apart (ab-fc 00:32, ab-fc2 00:40) already span 1.5 %/1.3 % and the 0.7 runs were a different day - but the documentation gap is real and the fix is one clause. The report table itself is not in the repo; only its numbers were checked.
- **Materiality verifier: confirmed** [medium] Recomputed from sweep.jsonl: T16-142 at T=0.7 (strata-T16-142-short/long-r1..r3, 16 users) = 466.9/342.1; the same config greedy (ab-fc-/ab-fc2-T16-142) = 469.7/349.4 -> protocol step +0.60 % / +2.14 %. Tabled 0.9.0->0.10.0 step 493/467 = +5.6 %, 357/342 = +4.4 %; same-protocol (#49) 492.95/469.7 = +4.95 %, 356.95/349.4 = +2.16 %. CHANGELOG.md:63 records the protocol switch, :53 ('was 470 / 349') does not say the baseline was re-measured greedy; TESTLOG.md:4-5 still defines T=0.7. Caveat: the greedy T16-142 pair itself spans 1.5 % (473.2/466.2), so part of the +2.1 % is session noise, not protocol alone. Fix is 15 min, no GPU; it changes a reported lever magnitude (fixed-cost long +2.2 %, not +4.4 %), not a default.
  - Corrected claim: The 0.9.0->0.10.0 step mixes T=0.7/3-rep (467/342) with greedy/2-run (470/349) baselines; the same-protocol fixed-cost gain is +5.0 % short / +2.2 % long, and the protocol+session difference accounts for most of the long-prompt gap (+2.1 %, within the 1.5 % greedy repeat span).

### [high] 'Ceiling ~600-620 tok/s pure decode at 16 slots without EP/TP rebuild' is the pre-0.12.0 measurement, already exceeded by the default
*key `ceiling-claim-unsupported` · inconsistency · status: **confirmed**, reviewer confidence 0.9*

**Claim.** Verdict: unsupported as a ceiling. 600-620 matches the engine-level decode rate measured BEFORE the split change (#3: 603 tok/s on 0.1.41; #55: 616.6 rows/s for T16-142fca). The current default (12,24,37 + MTP batch) decodes 653.6 rows/s at 16 slots, the uncommitted self-commit prototype 684.7, and PROFILE-next-levers.md projects ~720 with exact in-engine levers 4-5 and names its own bound as the 4-card round trip (24.6 ms -> 6.15 ms per stage-window ~ 650 rows/s), which lever 4 shortens further (5.85 ms measured). None of this needs EP/TP. HTTP throughput is ~80 % of D (522/654), so the headroom statement in the report should be 'D is 654 today, projected ~720; HTTP follows at ~0.8 x D for short prompts'.

**Evidence.** linux/TESTLOG.md #3, #55; linux/results/profile2/dec_rows.txt (616.6); linux/results/levers/self-commit/d16_ref/events.log via profile2/dec_rows.py -> 653.6 rows/s, stage-window 6.12 ms; d16_sc -> 684.7, 5.85 ms; linux/upstream/PROFILE-next-levers.md:72-73 ('fully balanced split at most +13 %'), :298-302 (round-trip bound 6.15 ms), :330-333 ('Decode goes from about 616 to about 720').

**Recommendation.** Replace the ceiling sentence in the report with the measured D progression (603 -> 616.6 -> 653.6 -> 684.7 prototype) and PROFILE's projection; if a hard ceiling is wanted, derive it from the round-trip formula with the lever-4 window (sum of four stage windows + sampling + hand-offs) and state the assumptions. 15 minutes.

**Done well (this lens):**
- TESTLOG #49 compares greedy against greedy (469.7/349.4 -> 493.0/357.0), so the fixed-cost gain itself is measured on one protocol; only the cross-version table inherits the break.
- CHANGELOG numbers round consistently from the TESTLOG means and the ab-*.out files everywhere checked (0.6.0, 0.9.0, 0.10.0, 0.11.0, 0.12.0).
- Every lever change has a documented exactness gate that was actually run (batch_test16/32, batch_overlap_test incl. RESUME, R dumps byte-identical for lever 3, gdn_parity 0 failures for lever 4) and the dumps are kept under results/.
- PROFILE-next-levers.md's lever-1 prediction (+6 % decode, ~655 rows/s) is confirmed by the measured 653.6 rows/s of the current default - the profiling model works.
- Engine-level timing (pf_reads.py, dec_rows.py) is used for the sub-2 % levers instead of HTTP single runs, which is the right instrument given the measured noise floor.
- #51 (batched admission) and #55 (profile) are in TESTLOG with their result directories; the 'Document every experiment' rule was followed up to #57.
- The fixed-cost shortfalls (-25 vs -40 ms planned, 7 vs 22 ms for the merged window) are reported honestly in #48 and README 297-298 rather than hidden.
- ab_quick.sh documents its protocol and the rationale for one run per arm in its header; per-arm status-*.json and server-*.out exist for every short arm.
- The engine log marker lines ('engine started: <full command line>') make every past run's exe, args, split and batch size reconstructible even though the log is cumulative.

**Points the verifiers added:**
- The batch_interleave_test exactness gate has never passed its BYIELD step in ANY recorded run, not just in the lever-4 arms: results/exact_direct_interleave.txt and exact_hostcopy_interleave.txt (#28, 0.1.41), overlap/m2 and overlap/exactness-r1 il-*.out, rebase142/gpu/il*.out (#45), fixedcost/{step0-o1,ab,k1k2,s1,s2,final}/il-*.out (#48; every summary.txt shows rc=1 for the il arms) and levers/self-commit/il_*.out all end 'D gave way: None' + 'engine: ERR BGEN: no such free slot (--batch 3)'; grep for 'YIELDED' over results/ finds nothing. Only steps 1-3 of the 7 scenarios in Strata-142/tools/batch_interleave_test.py:1-19 ever execute (A, B, C beside decoding, A's next turn); steps 4-7 (BYIELD give-way, BSTOP -> solo continuation, F from checkpoint) are untested, yet TESTLOG #28/#37/#40/#48 ('interleave identical', 'interleave = reference') and README:200 present it as passing. The harness is identical in Strata/ and Strata-142/ (diff empty); the ERR comes from generate.cpp:10091 because D is admitted (BADM 2) without honoring the harness's immediate 'BYIELD 2', so the second 'BGEN 2 D' hits an active slot. Consequence: the BYIELD path (patch 0023 STRATA_PIPE_BYIELD_NODRAIN, on in the default) has no exactness coverage; the harness or the engine's BYIELD timing needs a documented fix before levers 4-5 are gated on it.
- Raw data cited by TESTLOG is gitignored: .gitignore ignores '*.log' and '*.out' (exempting only /logs/), so all 13 results/*.out files named in the Raw-data column (ab-fc.out, ab-fc2.out, ab-lv.out, ab-mix.out, ab-pf*.out, ab-fcb.out, series_*.out), all 176 .out and 173 .log files under linux/results (engine-*.log, server-*.out) are not in git (git check-ignore confirms; git ls-files linux/results = 685 files, 0 .out, 0 .log). sweep.jsonl and the per-level .txt files are tracked so the numbers survive a clone, but the engine logs - the only record of each run's exact command line and split - and the proposed results/levers logs would be lost.
- No engine binary carries its commit: Strata/engine/BUILD.json describes only the 0.1.41 'strata' build, and the engine log's 'engine started' line has no version/commit. strata-g3-142fc (the default's exe) was built 2026-10-12 00:09:33, four minutes before c9bbd1fc (patch 0028 'review fixes for the admission switches') was committed at 00:13:58 - nothing records whether the shipped default includes patch 0028. strata-g3-142lv was rebuilt at 03:36:09 after the lever-4 measurements of 03:16-03:22; d16_sc/d32_ref/d32_sc were re-run at 03:43-03:45 (686.0/714.1/752.6 rows/s), so figures quoted from the earlier logs (684.7/711.6) no longer correspond to any file.
- TESTLOG.md:5 and README.md:69 define 'short' as about 120 prompt tokens, but every short-prompt row in sweep.jsonl has prompt_tok_avg 162-178 (first strata-G16-short 162, strata-T16-* 163-171, current ab-fcb 168-169, vLLM p100 170-172); the header has been wrong since the first run.
- TESTLOG.md:96 'Parked: dynamic switching pipeline <-> expert parallel (see the memory/ideas note in the README section)' points to a README section that does not exist (grep 'expert parallel|ideas' in linux/README.md hits only the vLLM --enable-expert-parallel flag at :130).
- Single-user long-prompt decode may have regressed with STRATA_SPLIT_MTP_BATCH=1 (default since 0.12.0): in the back-to-back ab-fcb run, 1 user, 1.2K prompts, identical greedy outputs, fcb decoded 119.3/124.2 tok/s with 'drafts accepted 276 of 488, 281 of 450' (59.4 %) vs fca 124.6/138.6 with 283/444, 315/439 (67.7 %); dec_p50 121.8 vs 131.7 (-7.5 %). Short prompts: 65.2 % vs 63.8 % (unchanged, which is all TESTLOG #57 checked: 'draft acceptance unchanged' from the 1-user short level). Upstream Strata-142/docs/MULTI_GPU.md:109-110 says this switch computes the draft K/V via Q8_1 x Q8_0 MMQ 'so the drafts, and how many are accepted, can move'. n=2 paired requests, so not conclusive; check with ~5 min GPU (T16-142fcb with and without the env, 1 user, 6-8 long requests, greedy, or tools/split_mtp_batch_parity.py). Decision impact: the switch buys +2.7 % at 16 users on 1.2K prompts (#56: 386.4 vs 376.2) and -37 ms per read, but a 4-10 % single-user loss would argue for leaving it out of the agent/single-user config.
- The lever-4 numbers are being regenerated right now and the negative GDN result is unlogged: strata-g3-142lv was rebuilt 03:36:09 after the STRATA_GDN_SLOTS code was dropped (results/levers/self-commit/with-gdn-slots.patch, 229 lines; run.sh:2-3 'dropped - no gain': d16_on 680.4 vs d16_sc 684.7 rows/s). run8.log re-runs every gate and timing with the final binary (bt16_sc/bt32_sc/ov_sc rc=0, il_sc rc=1 as always, d16_ref 653.7, d16_sc 686.0 = +4.9 %, d32_ref 714.1, d32_sc running at 03:44). The TESTLOG row for lever 4 must quote run8, and the multi-slot GDN step (PROFILE decode lever #6, 'do with #2') deserves its own 'no gain' line so it is not re-tried.
- The interleave test's phase 4 (D yields via BYIELD, E is admitted, both compared to solo) has never run to completion in any logged run: results/exact_direct_interleave.txt (#28, 0.1.41), overlap/exactness-r1/il-ref.out, fixedcost/s1/il-all.out and levers/self-commit/il_*.out all end with 'D gave way: None' then 'ERR BGEN: no such free slot (--batch 3) or a bad max_new' (Strata-142/tools/batch_interleave_test.py:142-156 sends BGEN 2 + BYIELD 2 on an engine started with --batch 3). TESTLOG #28/#37/#40/#45/#48 'interleave identical / = reference' therefore cover phases 1-3 only; BYIELD mid-read is covered by batch_overlap_test, so no exactness gap for the defaults, but the wording should say so (one line) or the test's phase 4 be fixed.
- Protocol definition is wrong on prompt length: TESTLOG.md:5 and README.md:69 say 'short ≈ 120 prompt tokens', but every short arm measured 163-170 (sweep.jsonl prompt_tok_avg: strata-T16-trim-short 164, strata-T16-142-short-r1 168, ab-fcb-T16-142fcb-short 169, vllm031-flashnext-pc-p100 170); old and new runs agree, so it is not a protocol change, but the stated load profile is 40 % off and PROFILE's read-time model uses 150 tokens. 2 min.
- Strata-142/serve/server.py:2308 (PARALLEL_MAX = 8) and :2327 print '"parallel": 16 - the engine runs at most 8 requests together; it will use 8' into every server-*.out although the patched engine runs 16 slots (marker line '--batch 16'; 16-user TTFT pattern and 35 tok/s per user confirm 16 concurrent). Harmless, but misleading in the logs and in the upstream PR, where patch 0001 lifts the engine limit without updating the server text.
- Results convention limits what 'git add results/levers/' can achieve: .gitignore:24-25 drops *.log and *.out repo-wide (only /logs/ exempt), so results/profile2 holds 30 tracked files and 0 logs, and the events.log/driver.out that dec_rows.py and pf_reads.py read (the only source of the 616.6/653.7/686.0 rows/s figures) will never be in git. The durable record of engine-level numbers is the TESTLOG row plus notes.txt, which makes writing #58-#60 the material step rather than the git add.


## Lens: plan

### [medium] Lever 3 (MMQ group sort): GO; expect +5.4 % long / +1.0 % short through HTTP, confirm on the long arm only
*key `lever3-go-translation` · confirmation · status: **confirmed**, reviewer confidence 0.85*

**Claim.** The measured -65.2 ms (1.2K) / -9.6 ms (150) idle read savings translate, with the document's own model, into about +18-21 tok/s on the 1.2K arm but only +4.6-5.1 tok/s on the short arm; the short gain is at the single-run noise level and cannot be confirmed by one A/B, the long gain can.

**Evidence.** linux/results/levers/mmq-sort/notes.txt: t_ref 145.8/428.6 ms -> t_gs 136.2/363.4 ms; bitwise: prefill_mmq_tab_test 890/890, R dumps d_ref vs d_gs byte-identical, batch_test16 16/16 and bt16-gs.json == auxcheck/bt16-fcb.json. Model thr^2/L: with the doc's coefficients (PROFILE-next-levers.md 'Throughput model': 0.276 and 0.475 tok/s per ms) -> +18.0 tok/s long, +4.6 short; re-derived on the current default (405.3^2/512 = 0.321/ms, 522.4^2/512 = 0.533/ms) -> +20.9 (405.3 -> 426.2, +5.2 %) and +5.1 (522.4 -> 527.5, +1.0 %). Equivalent form 512/(512/D + R_adm) with D = 653.6 (d16_ref) gives 427.3 / 527.6. Single-run spread 0.5-1 % (statistics lens) = 2-5 tok/s short, 2-4 tok/s long.

**Recommendation.** Run one ab_quick arm (T16-142fcb + STRATA_MMQ_GROUP_SORT=1, engine strata-g3-142lv; ~10 min). Decision rule: long arm >= +3 % (>= 417 tok/s) confirms; short arm only has to be 'not worse' (>= 517). Do not spend 3 repetitions trying to resolve the +1 % short gain. Then promote to the default (T16-142fcc) together with lever 4 if that passes too.

- **Facts verifier: confirmed** [medium] results/levers/mmq-sort/notes.txt: t_ref 145.8/428.6 ms -> t_gs 136.2/363.4 ms (-9.6/-65.2) as claimed; exactness chain verified with cmp: bt16-sc.json == bt16-gs.json == auxcheck/bt16-fcb.json, cmp.txt d_ref vs d_gs bitwise identical, prefill_mmq_tab_test 890/0 failed. Model recomputed from TESTLOG #57 (522.4/405.3) and D=653.6: R_adm = 512/522.4-512/653.6 = 0.1967 s, 512/405.3-0.7834 = 0.4799 s; minus idle reads = 50.9/51.3 ms fixed; lever 3 -> 512/(0.7834+0.1871) = 527.6 and 512/(0.7834+0.4147) = 427.4 (+1.0 %/+5.4 %). PROFILE-next-levers.md:108-109 really carries 0.276/0.475 tok/s per ms -> 18.0/4.6 tok/s. Noise from sweep.jsonl 16-user repeats: 3-rep sd 0.17-0.83 % (T16-142 short 469.9/468.3/462.5; overlap-off 441.1/439.4/441.9), two-run pairs up to 1.5 % (ab-fc/ab-fc2 T16-142 473.2/466.2) -> the +5 tok/s short gain is 1-2 sd, the +22 tok/s long gain is 6-12 sd. Decision rule (>= 417 long, >= 517 short) is sound. Note ab_quick runs both prompt lengths per server start anyway, so 'long arm only' is a decision rule, not a time saving; the current strata-g3-142lv binary (03:36) also contains lever 4 (commit 7e7e169e) env-gated off, which is fine for an opt-in A/B.
- **Materiality verifier: confirmed** [medium] Re-checked results/levers/mmq-sort/notes.txt (t_ref 145.8/428.6 -> t_gs 136.2/363.4 ms = -9.6/-65.2), prefill_mmq_tab_test.txt ('890 comparisons, 0 failed'), cmp.txt (d_ref vs d_gs rall 60,723,200 B bitwise identical), and `cmp bt16-gs.json auxcheck/bt16-fcb.json` (identical). Model recomputed from TESTLOG #57 and the re-run d16_ref (653.7 rows/s): R_adm = 196.9 ms short / 480.0 ms long; lever 3 alone gives 512/(512/653.7+0.1873) = 527.6 and 512/(0.7833+0.4148) = 427.4, i.e. +1.0 % / +5.5 %. Noise from sweep.jsonl: in-session repeats (#41 arms) range 0.3-0.7 %, same-config cross-session 0.1-1.5 % (T16-142 473.2 vs 466.2 short, 351.6 vs 347.2 long in ab-fc/ab-fc2; fca 492.9 vs 490.5), so the +1 % short gain is unresolvable and the +5 % long gain is not. Caveat: the proposed thresholds compare against #57's numbers from another session; a same-session fcb reference arm (+5 min) is the stronger design, and a 3-arm session (fcb, +3, +3+4; ~15 min) settles both levers at once.

### [medium] The '600-620 pure decode' ceiling is stale: the default already decodes 653.6 rows/s; the HTTP model fits with a 51 ms fixed admission cost
*key `ceiling-stale-model-ok` · inconsistency · status: **confirmed**, reviewer confidence 0.9*

**Claim.** DESIGN-overlap.md and the older analyses quote a 603-640 rows/s decode ceiling, but the current default (12,24,37) measures 653.6 rows/s at 16 slots and 711.6 at 32 (self-commit lifts these to 684.7 / 754.3). With T_adm -> 0 the model gives thr -> D = 654 (+25 % short, +61 % long), which is unreachable; the practical admission floor (the read itself, ~100 / ~330 ms) caps HTTP at roughly 580 / 460. The arithmetic of the plan is otherwise sound and its lever estimates have so far landed at 96-120 % of forecast, so the plan's blanket 50-70 % realism discount is too pessimistic.

**Evidence.** dec_rows.py on results/levers/self-commit: d16_ref 653.6 rows/s (stage window 6.12 ms), d16_sc 684.7 (5.85), d32_ref 711.6 (11.25), d32_sc 754.3 (10.62). Fit from TESTLOG #57 (522.4 / 405.3): R_adm = 512/522.4 - 512/653.6 = 0.1967 s short; 512/405.3 - 0.7834 = 0.4799 s long; minus idle reads 145.8 / 428.6 ms (mmq-sort/notes.txt) = 51 ms fixed cost in both cases, matching the doc's '~50 ms (drain ~22, first window ~27, checkpoint ~5.5)'. Stale text: upstream/DESIGN-overlap.md:379 'D ~ 620', :409 '(603-640)'; ANALYSIS-batching-levers.md:172 '603 tok/s decode ceiling'. Forecast vs measured: lever 1+2 planned +4.6/+5.4 % -> #57 +6.5/+7.3 %; lever 3 planned -40..-55 ms -> -65 ms; lever 4 planned +5 % D -> +4.8 %. Projection from the fcb base: levers 3+4 -> 548 / 440; +lever 5 (-0.33 ms on 5.85 ms, D ~725) -> 573 / 457.

**Recommendation.** Replace the ceiling sentences with 'D = 653.6 rows/s at 16 slots (142fcb), 684.7 with self-commit; HTTP = 512/(512/D + R_adm), R_adm = read + 51 ms' in TESTLOG/README (15 min). Use this fitted form for every future estimate instead of per-ms coefficients from the 142fca base. Drop the 50-70 % discount in planning; use the measured 1:1 track record.

- **Facts verifier: confirmed** [low] Stale lines verified exactly: upstream/DESIGN-overlap.md:379 'D ≈ 620 rows/s of pure decode', :409 'pure decode (603-640)'; upstream/ANALYSIS-batching-levers.md:172 '603 tok/s decode ceiling' (also :213, :225, :261); plus README.md:217 '(instead of 620)'. D recomputed with results/profile2/dec_rows.py: final-build back-to-back run (run8, 03:40-03:45) d16_ref 653.7, d16_sc 686.0, d32_ref 714.1, d32_sc 752.6 rows/s (the lens's 653.6/684.7/711.6/754.3 are the first-round files, since overwritten; notes.txt records both). The 51 ms fixed cost falls out of the fit in both arms (50.9/51.3 ms) and matches PROFILE:107 'about 50 ms (drain ~22, first window ~27, checkpoint ~5.5)'. Two embellishments do not hold: (a) the 'landed at 96-120 % of forecast' track record is really 96-160 % (levers 1+2 planned +4.6/+5.4-5.9 % per the merged table -> #57 +6.5/+7.3 % = 110-141 %; lever 3 planned -40..-55 / -5..-9 ms -> -65.2/-9.6 = 118-192 %; lever 4 +5 % -> +4.8-4.9 %), which only strengthens the conclusion that the 50-70 % discount (PROFILE Corrections item 4, 'Realistically, expect about +8 to +12 %') is too pessimistic; (b) the '~100 / ~330 ms practical read floor -> 580/460' is an unsupported assumption (no measurement of a read floor exists; #51's shortest read is 68 tok in 100 ms). The recommendation is a 15-min doc fix plus a modelling convention; it changes estimates, not the next lever.
- **Materiality verifier: confirmed** [low] dec_rows.py on the re-run logs: d16_ref 653.7 rows/s (6.12 ms window), d16_sc 686.0, d32_ref 714.1, d32_sc 752.6. Fit: 512/522.4-512/653.7 = 196.9 ms, 512/405.3-0.7833 = 480.0 ms; minus idle reads 145.8/428.6 -> 51.1 / 51.4 ms fixed cost, matching the doc's '~50 ms'. Stale lines verified: DESIGN-overlap.md:379 'D ≈ 620', :409 '(603-640)', ANALYSIS-batching-levers.md:172 '603 tok/s decode ceiling'. Forecast record verified against PROFILE-next-levers.md: levers 1+2 planned +4.6/+5.4-5.9 % -> #57 +6.5/+7.3 %; lever 3 planned -40..-55 ms -> -65 ms; lever 4 planned +5 % -> +4.9 %. Projections recomputed: 3+4 -> 548/441; +lever 5 (-0.33 ms on 5.83) -> ~573/457. Actionable but it changes only the planning model and three doc lines (no GPU time, no change to the lever order), hence low.

### [medium] Upstream --pipeline-windows is off with --batch and HIP-only by default: no rebase before lever 5, and it does not substitute lever 5
*key `pipeline-windows-not-applicable` · inconsistency · status: **confirmed**, reviewer confidence 0.95*

**Claim.** The 0.1.43 '+7-12 %' comes from a speculative single-conversation pipeline (stage 0 guesses the next window while the last stage verifies); the source hard-disables it with --batch slots and auto-enables it only under STRATA_USE_HIP. It attacks turn-taking idle time of ONE conversation, not the 1.25 ms imbalance idle of the 4-group batch pipeline, so it neither replaces nor stacks with lever 5. The rest of the 72 upstream commits contains nothing for a fully resident CUDA batch path; the sm_86 Q3_K kernel is moot because the pack has no Q3_K tensors.

### [high] Cheapest unexplored lever: the 3090s run decode in P2 at 1785 MHz SM / 9501 MHz memory, far below the 380 W limit; lock clocks (root) and log them
*key `clocks-p2-cheap-lever` · suggestion · status: **weakened**, reviewer confidence 0.6*

**Claim.** Power is not the limiter (limit already at the 380 W maximum, draw 117-133 W during a 32-slot profile run, no throttle reasons), but the cards sit in P2 with the memory clock 2.6 % below P0 and the SM clock at 1785 MHz against 2115 max (1965 at idle). Roughly half of a decode window is launch/latency-bound (GDN 7.7 us launches, attention 41 us per row, HC gr_* on 41-82 blocks, head) and the prefill MMQ is compute-bound, so a locked higher SM clock could give several percent on both reads and windows for ~30 min of work, and nothing in the scripts records clocks today.

**Evidence.** nvidia-smi 03:32 during p32_sc: all 4 GPUs pstate P2, clocks.sm 1785 MHz, clocks.mem 9501 MHz, power.draw 117.9-133.0 W, power.limit 380 W (default 370, max 380), clocks_event_reasons.active 0x0; 03:25 snapshot: idle cards at 1965-1980 MHz, GPU0 in P0 at 9751 MHz memory, max SM 2115. grep clocks.sm/power.draw/nvidia-smi in linux/ab_quick.sh, run_strata_variant.sh, bench_concurrency.py, bench_multiturn.py: no hits. PROFILE: routed experts at 735-860 GB/s (memory-bound, scales with the memory clock), MMQ 57 % of a 512 chunk (compute-bound), per-row attention 41 us, gdn_step_norm_multi 7.7 us x 72 launches (latency-bound).

**Recommendation.** (1) Add 'nvidia-smi --query-gpu=index,pstate,clocks.sm,clocks.mem,power.draw,clocks_throttle_reasons.active --format=csv -lms 200 > results/clocks-<label>.csv' to run_strata_variant.sh (10 min, zero run cost) so the next A/B shows the clock distribution under load. (2) When the GPUs are free: sudo nvidia-smi -lgc 1965 (then 2100) and -lmc 9751 on all cards, pf_driver decode mode (30 s) + pf_reads 150/1.2K vs reference, then one ab_quick arm; reset with -rgc / -rmc. Exact by construction (same kernels). Expected 0 to +5 %; also cuts run-to-run variance from boost behaviour. Total ~45 min.

- **Facts verifier: refuted** [low] I sampled nvidia-smi at 03:41:34 during the d16_ref decode-only stretch (run8: d16_ref start 03:40:37, driver.out STRETCH at +32.9 s to +62.9 s, i.e. 03:41:10-03:41:40, 16 slots): all four GPUs at 377.6-378.4 W of the 380 W limit, pstate P2, SM 1830-1905 MHz, clocks_event_reasons.active 0x4 (SW power cap) on every card, utilization 87-100 %, memory.used 18.7-20.3 GiB (the Strata engine). So during batch decode the SM clock is set by the power limiter, not by P2 behaviour; -lgc 1965/2100 cannot be sustained and -lmc 9751 would only move power from SM to memory. The lens's 117-133 W / 0x0 sample must have fallen outside a decode stretch (p32_sc's stretch ended ~03:32:28, the arm at 03:32:36; my 03:47:57-03:48:05 samples between arms show 156-192 W at 20-34 % util, and 03:49:49 shows P8 idle). Scripts indeed record no clocks (grep nvidia-smi/clocks in ab_quick.sh, run_strata_variant.sh, bench_*.py: none), and that part of the recommendation remains worth 10 min: a per-arm log of pstate/clocks/power/throttle reasons would have caught this and also shows whether the second (long) arm of each server start runs warmer than the first.
  - Corrected claim: The 3090s are power-capped during 16-slot decode (377-378 W of 380 W, SW-power-cap flag on all four cards, SM 1830-1905 MHz), so clock locking is not a throughput lever and 'expected 0 to +5 %' has no basis; keep only the 10-min 'log pstate/clocks/power/throttle reasons per arm' change, which also documents the power-cap noise source.
- **Materiality verifier: confirmed** [medium] Reproduced live at 03:48 while the 142lv lever test (pid 2978590) was running: all four cards P2, clocks.sm 1785 MHz (max 2115), clocks.mem 9501 MHz (max 9751), power 158-187 W of a 380 W limit, clocks_event_reasons 0x0; 20 s later, idle, P8 at 210 MHz. No clock logging exists: grep of ab_quick.sh, run_strata_variant.sh, bench_*.py, series_*.sh and start-strata.sh finds no nvidia-smi/clock query; G3's server_analytics.py records power/utilization/memory but no clock field; Strata-142/serve/telemetry.py reads only nvmlDeviceGetPowerUsage. The latency-bound share of the window (QSA 0.89, GDN 0.95, HC gr_* 1.25 of ~5.8 ms) is consistent with the profile. Cost is right: logging is ~10 min with no GPU time; the lock experiment is ~45 min of GPU and needs root (-lgc works on Ampere GeForce; -lmc on GeForce is uncertain, so the 2.6 % memory-clock part may be unavailable). I rate it medium rather than high because the gain is unproven (0 to +5 %) and P2 with a reduced memory clock is the standard CUDA compute state on GeForce, but per GPU-minute it is the cheapest open lever and the logging should be added before the next A/B.

### [medium] 24 slots: ~+4.5 % at 24 users, nothing at 16 users, -30 % per-user speed; VRAM still fits but FP16 dense copies would leave 1.2 GB on CUDA2
*key `t24-only-for-more-users` · suggestion · status: **weakened**, reviewer confidence 0.75*

**Claim.** Interpolating the measured per-row cost gives D24 ~690 rows/s (727 with self-commit), which lifts throughput only when 24 users are actually active; at 16 users the groups resolve to 4 active rows and T24 equals T16. It is a product decision (fairness vs aggregate), not a 16-user default candidate, and #47's +2 % is consistent with today's model.

**Evidence.** 142fcb: 16 slots 6.12 ms at 4 rows, 32 slots 11.25 ms at 8 rows -> 1.28 ms/row, 1.0 ms fixed -> 6 rows 8.69 ms -> 690 rows/s. HTTP at 24 users: 512/(512/690 + 0.1967) = 546 (+4.5 % vs 522.4), long 512/(0.742 + 0.4799) = 419 (+3.4 %). Raw per-user decode 690/24 = 28.8 vs 653.6/16 = 40.9 rows/s; #47 client-side 21 vs 31 tok/s. BATCHING.md:141 'consecutive requests land in different pipeline groups' and the g3 variable-S group path (ANALYSIS 'G.S (8610)') -> 4-row windows at 16 users. VRAM: engine-ab-fcb log 'slot sessions 0.15 GiB each' and free 4222/5543/3883/4481 MiB at capture; +8 slots = -1.2 GiB -> 3.0/4.3/2.7/3.3 GB; FP16 dense copies -1.44 GB -> 1.6/2.9/1.2/1.8 GB. T24-142 log: 6144/6144 profiled pairs on all cards (residency held at 24 slots).

**Recommendation.** Only if the deployment expects >16 concurrent users: one ab_quick with LEVELS=24 for T16-142fcb vs a T24-142fcb (10 min), expected +4 %. Do not change the 16-user default. If 24 slots and the FP16 dense copies are both wanted, implement the plan's VRAM budget env first. 32 slots (754 rows/s measured) likewise only for >= 32 users (~580 short at 32 users).

### [low] Overlap middle ground: gate STRATA_PIPE_OVERLAP on prompt length (long reads only); pays only under mixed traffic
*key `overlap-length-gate` · suggestion · status: **refuted**, reviewer confidence 0.6*

**Recommendation.** Only if the MIX reference (#53) is the metric that matters: add an engine env STRATA_PIPE_OVERLAP_MIN_TOKENS (the gate already knows the read length; ~1-2 h) and run two MIX arms (20 min). Expected +4-6 % long in MIX, 0 in lockstep. Not before levers 3 and 4.

But every arm had homogeneous prompt lengths, so there is no measurement of a mixed-length stream and 'a length gate keeps most of the long gain while leaving short TTFT untouched' is a hypothesis; the long reads' own TTFT cost (MIX long p95 7.12 -> 10.13 s) stays with a gate. It changes no current decision (explicitly 'not before levers 3 and 4', MIX is not the default metric) and the +4-6 % is a guess.
  - Corrected claim: A STRATA_PIPE_OVERLAP_MIN_TOKENS gate is an untested hypothesis for mixed-length traffic only; the overlap's measured gain (+6-7 % long in MIX / at 24 users, 0 to -1 % in lockstep at 16) and its p95 cost are real, but no data shows what a length gate would keep. Backlog item, not part of the current plan.
- **Materiality verifier: refuted** [low] The numbers are right (#41 436.8/327.7 vs 440.8/327.1, 24 users +2/+7 %, p95 +20-25 %; #53 419.3/308.6 vs 409.8/290.3 with p50 0.21 -> 0.34 / 0.54 -> 1.35 s; #44 queue gate worse; #42 PRIO=0 within 1-2 %), but the recommended validation cannot show anything: bench_concurrency.py takes one --prompt-tok per run (only reply lengths are mixed via --max-tok-min), so a ~512-token gate equals 'overlap on' in the 1.2K arm and 'off' in the short arm and the two MIX arms would reproduce #53. Showing the gate's value needs a new mixed-prompt-length benchmark mode plus 1-2 h of engine work, for a gain that exists only under mixed traffic. Nothing to decide before levers 3-5; the lens says so itself.
  - Corrected claim: A prompt-length gate for STRATA_PIPE_OVERLAP can only be evaluated with a benchmark that mixes prompt lengths within one run (bench_concurrency.py has none today); the existing MIX arms would just repeat #53. Park it.

### [low] KV fp16 instead of int8 is not a decode lever here
*key `kv-fp16-drop` · suggestion · status: **weakened**, reviewer confidence 0.75*

**Claim.** Decode attention is launch/latency-bound, not KV-bandwidth-bound, at these context lengths, so the KV element type cannot change the 0.89 ms QSA share measurably; fp16 would double KV VRAM and change bits. Lever 5 addresses the actual inefficiency.

**Evidence.** PROFILE decode: attn_chunk_kernel 41 us per row per QSA layer, 'runs once per row'; QSA proj+attention 0.89 ms of 5.7 ms per window; contexts in the benchmark ~170+512 or ~1230+512 tokens -> int8 K+V per row per layer well under 1 MB (~1 us at 700 GB/s). Slot sessions 0.15 GiB per card each (engine log) would grow with fp16 KV.

**Recommendation.** Drop; no test needed. Spend the time on lever 5 (multi-slot QSA), which batches those per-row launches.

- **Facts verifier: refuted** [low] grep for fp16/f16 KV in linux/README.md, TESTLOG.md, upstream/*.md and Strata-142/docs/BATCHING.md finds no proposal to switch --kv int8 to fp16; the configs use --kv int8 and no lever list mentions it. The technical reasoning is fine (PROFILE:235: attn_chunk_kernel 41 us per row per QSA layer, 'runs once per row'; QSA 0.89 ms of 5.7), though 'well under 1 MB per row per layer' is unverified (int8 K+V for ~1.7K tokens is ~1-3 MB depending on KV heads x head dim, still a few us at 700 GB/s). Dropping a lever nobody proposed changes no decision.
  - Corrected claim: No fp16-KV lever exists in the repo or the plan; nothing to drop. Decode attention is latency-bound (41 us per row), so the KV element type is not where lever 5 (multi-slot QSA) gets its gain.
- **Materiality verifier: confirmed** [low] PROFILE-next-levers.md: attn_chunk_kernel 41 µs per row per QSA layer, 'runs once per row'; vprof p16_ref shows QSA proj+attention 0.89-0.91 ms per window on every stage, unchanged by any arm. At the benchmark's 0.7-1.75K contexts the int8 K+V a row reads per layer is of the order of 1-2 MB (a few µs at ~700 GB/s), so the kernel is latency-bound and the element type cannot move the 0.89 ms; fp16 would also double the 0.15 GiB per slot session (fca log) and change bits. Correct and it prevents a wasted arm, but no arm was scheduled, hence low.

### [medium] batch_interleave_test stops at step 4 in reference and lever arms alike since the rebase: the yield-then-continue and slot-to-solo paths are unverified for lever 4
*key `interleave-test-gap` · risk · status: **weakened**, reviewer confidence 0.7*

**Claim.** Both il_ref and il_on end with 'ERR BGEN: no such free slot (--batch 3)' before step 4 (D gives way via BYIELD, E admitted while D waits, D continues from its slot, step 6 back to the solo path); the team accepts 'identical to the reference up to step 4', but self-commit changes exactly the per-row keep/snapshot/PLE-history ordering that parked and resumed slots depend on, and batch_overlap_test covers BYIELD-during-read and a parked slot but not a slot that yields and later continues.

**Evidence.** results/levers/self-commit/il_ref.out, il_on.out, il_ref_min.out: 'D gave way: None' then the ERR; run2-4.log rc=1 for all three. results/fixedcost/final/notes.txt:6 'all stop at step 4 rc=1 like the reference'. Tool: Strata-142/tools/batch_interleave_test.py lines 148-175 (BGEN 2 D + BYIELD 2, expects YIELDED/BADM). batch_overlap_test.py:11-14, 326-355 (BYIELD during a read, parked slot over the group width, G2 RESUME).

**Recommendation.** Spend ~1 h to update the tool to the 0.1.42 slot semantics (free or BSTOP slot 2 before re-admitting D, or admit D into a free slot) so steps 4-6 run again, then re-run il_ref/il_on before lever 4 becomes the default. Cheap insurance for an exactness-critical change.

- **Facts verifier: confirmed** [medium] Every batch_interleave_test run since the 0.1.42 rebase ends identically with 'D gave way: None' then 'ERR BGEN: no such free slot (--batch 3)': results/rebase142/gpu/il.out and il-sc.out (#45), fixedcost/step0-o1/il-o1.out, fixedcost/s1/il-all.out, fixedcost/s2/il-on.out, fixedcost/final/notes.txt:6 ('all stop at step 4 rc=1 like the reference'), levers/self-commit il_ref/il_ref_min/il_on/il_sc.out (run logs rc=1), and lever 4's notes.txt files it as 'pre-existing'. Tool lines 148-175 send BGEN 2 D + BYIELD 2 and expect YIELDED/BADM, then re-admit D into slot 2, which 0.1.42 rejects; step 6 (BSTOP after 50 tokens, GEN continues from the slot) never runs either. batch_overlap_test.py (docstring 11-14, 326-355) sends BYIELD only during an overlapped read, where the overlap path drops it (DESIGN-overlap: 'a BYIELD is dropped with no extra drain'), so yield-then-continue has had no exactness coverage through four default promotions (#45, #49, #52, #57), while the production server does send BYIELD (#48: 'the server's normal BYIELD costs 0'). Self-commit (7e7e169e) changes exactly the per-row keep/snapshot ordering a parked-and-resumed slot depends on, so the ~1 h tool fix before promoting T16-142fcc is well justified; the gap is broader than 'for lever 4'.
- **Materiality verifier: weakened** [medium] The failure is real and pre-dates the levers: il_ref.out, il_on.out, il_sc.out all end 'D gave way: None' + 'ERR BGEN: no such free slot (--batch 3)', rc=1 in run2-4/run8.log; rebase142/gpu/notes.txt:8 records it at #45 as 'pre-existing, reference engine stops the same way'. But the mechanism the lens worries about does not exist in the production path: il_ref.engine.log shows D's 4890-token read completing in one piece ('read in 1097 ms'); include/strata/program/batch_read.hpp:24 returns one piece whenever `piped` is true, and generate.cpp:10000/10063 drop a BYIELD in the pipelined pump ('a pipelined read never gives way'), so with the 4-stage pipeline a read never yields and the tool's re-sent 'BGEN 2 D' hits a slot that is already decoding. The real coverage loss since #45 is steps 6-7 (slot -> solo continuation after BSTOP, which the server does for a request left alone, server.py:1222-1241; F from a turn checkpoint), which no other tool covers; slot state after self-commit is partly covered by step 3 (A's next turn IDENTICAL) and ov_sc's G2 RESUME. The fix is cheaper than 'update to new slot semantics': make step 4 expect no yield under a pipeline (or skip it) so 6-7 run; ~1.5 min of GPU per rerun.
  - Corrected claim: batch_interleave_test stops at step 4 because the pipelined engine never takes a BYIELD (batch_read.hpp:24, generate.cpp:10000), not because of slot semantics; the unverified production paths since #45 are steps 6-7 (slot-to-solo continuation, checkpoint continuation). Patch the tool to skip the yield expectation under a pipeline and rerun il_ref/il_sc before lever 4 becomes the default.

"Not bit-identical at temperature 0.7" is wrong'; TESTLOG.md lines 86-97 ('Open / next steps') still list 'Rebase onto upstream 0.1.42 (+235 commits)' although #45 did it; DESIGN-overlap.md:379 'D ≈ 620' and :409 '603-640' against the measured 653.7 rows/s. Housekeeping for the next TESTLOG commit, no GPU, no decision.

**Done well (this lens):**
- Exactness discipline on lever 3 is exemplary: unit test 890/890, all-rows R dumps byte-identical, batch_test16 output byte-identical to the previous default's dump, and the non-exact zero-code probe (SORT_NE) was used only for timing.
- The throughput model is self-consistent: fitting 512/(512/D + R_adm) with the measured D = 653.6 reproduces a 51 ms fixed admission cost for both prompt lengths, matching the profiled drain + first window + checkpoint.
- Lever order was right: the two config-only levers (split 12,24,37, SPLIT_MTP_BATCH) were measured first and delivered +6.5/+7.3 %, with the 1-user acceptance check (130.9 vs 129.6 tok/s) ruling out a draft-quality regression.
- The plan's demotion of GPU3-only decode levers is confirmed by the new per-stage profile (GPU3 leads GPU0 by only 0.07 ms after the split).
- The no-go on the background MTP draft (GPU3 busy 233.7 of 237.4 ms) and on chunk-layout changes as non-exact are correctly argued from the traces.
- Lever 4 was built as opt-in with the exactness tools run before any throughput number was looked at, and every arm runs in its own systemd scope with identical prompts (PF_STAMP=fixed).

**Points the verifiers added:**
- The GPUs are power-capped during batch decode: nvidia-smi during d16_ref's 16-slot decode stretch (03:41:34) showed all four 3090s at 377.6-378.4 W of the 380 W limit, clocks_event_reasons 0x4 (SW power cap), SM 1830-1905 MHz. Beyond refuting the clock lever, this is an undocumented noise source (clock follows temperature/power state) and an argument for logging pstate/clocks/power per arm and for noting that the long arm of each ab_quick server start runs on warmer cards than the short arm.
- Lever-3/4 bookkeeping is not yet in the repo: results/levers/ (02:50-03:49) is untracked, TESTLOG.md ends at #57 with no rows for mmq-sort or self-commit, CHANGELOG [Unreleased] has none, and Strata-142 7e7e169e (03:49:00) is local only ('not pushed' per notes.txt). The project's own rule is to log each test before the next starts; the lens's docs finding did not mention it. (notes.txt in both lever folders is good interim documentation.)
- The two GO findings propose inconsistent A/B protocols (lever 3: one arm with GROUP_SORT alone; lever 4: 'combined with lever 3 in the same arm'). Since the lever-3 short gain (+1 %) is below single-run noise and the fcb reference (522.4/405.3) dates from 02:46, the cheapest attributable design is three back-to-back arms on one engine binary: T16-142fcb reference, +STRATA_MMQ_GROUP_SORT=1, +GROUP_SORT+STRATA_BATCH_SELF_COMMIT=1 (~15 min each), judged on the long arm.
- ab_quick.sh's header claim 'three repetitions differed by +-0.3 % (TESTLOG #41)' is the best case: from sweep.jsonl the 16-user 3-rep sd is 0.17-0.83 % (T16-142 short 469.9/468.3/462.5 = 1.6 % range) and two-run pairs reach 1.5 % (ab-fc vs ab-fc2 T16-142 473.2/466.2). Every single-run decision rule in the plan should use ~1 % (5 tok/s) as the resolution, which the lens's lever-3 rule happens to satisfy.
- The self-commit timing and profile artifacts the lens cites (d16/d32_*, p16/p32_*) were produced on an intermediate strata-g3-142lv build containing the later-dropped GDN-slots code (binary rebuilt 03:36); the team already re-ran the reference and self-commit decode arms plus all exactness gates on the final binary (run8/run9: 653.7 -> 686.0 and 714.1 -> 752.6 rows/s; bt16-sc == auxcheck/bt16-fcb; bt32-sc == bt32-off; ov_sc PASS; ov_off identical). This is handled well; the review should cite the final-build artifacts (notes.txt 03:49) rather than the first round.
- The vprof 'total stamped' figures used throughout the plan and the review are window-graph stamps only (verify.cpp:804 gpu_stamp inside the layer loop, summed in profile_report at verify.cpp:1727-1750); the commit graph (0.44 ms), GPU3's sampling (0.17 ms) and launch wait (0.11 ms) are outside them, which is why the stamped maximum (5.77 ms) is 0.35 ms short of the measured 6.12 ms window. Any stage-balance argument built on vprof totals alone (including the plan's) needs this correction.
- TESTLOG #57 reports the single-user check only for the short arm (130.9 vs 129.6); the long 1-user arm in ab-fcb.out moved 115.3 -> 108.7 agg (dec p50 131.7 -> 121.8) with 2 requests per level, while #46 measured 106.4 for the same arm, so 115.3 was likely the outlier. Not a regression, but the 1-user long figure should be quoted or the level dropped from the protocol, since 2 requests cannot resolve +-5 %.
- The decode numbers the lens quotes (653.6/684.7/711.6/754.3) came from the 03:16-03:24 runs, which were overwritten at 03:40-03:45 by a re-run with the final build; the current files give 653.7 / 686.0 / 714.1 / 752.6 rows/s (d16_on/d32_on unchanged at 680.4/751.3). The two runs agree within 0.2-0.35 %, which is the engine-level repeatability figure that justifies calling GDN_SLOTS (-0.8 %/-0.2 %) 'no gain' and should be the number logged in TESTLOG #58.
- ab_quick.sh's header claims '+-0.3 %' from #41, but that is the in-session spread (3 repeats: range 0.3-0.7 %, sd 0.14-0.29 %); the same config measured in different sessions differs by up to 1.5 % (sweep.jsonl: T16-142 short 473.2 vs 466.2 and long 351.6 vs 347.2 in ab-fc vs ab-fc2; fca 492.9 vs 490.5). Any decision rule must compare arms within one session; a 3-arm session (fcb reference, +lever 3, +levers 3+4; ~15 min) is the proportionate protocol for the two pending levers, and the lens's thresholds against #57 are weaker than that.
- The lever-4 code was committed only at 03:49 (Strata-142 7e7e169e) while the lens was writing; results/levers/ (mmq-sort and self-commit, ~1 MB of exactness and timing evidence), the modified linux/run_strata_variant.sh (--host ${BIND:-0.0.0.0}) and the with-gdn-slots.patch are still untracked/uncommitted and TESTLOG has no #58. Per the project's own rule the two lever sessions (02:50-03:49) should be logged and committed before the HTTP arm so the dec_rows/pf_reads numbers and the binary's commit id are not lost or re-measured.
- In the 4-stage pipeline the server's BYIELD is a no-op by construction (batch_read.hpp:24 one piece when piped; generate.cpp:10000/10063 drop it), so STRATA_PIPE_BYIELD_NODRAIN=1 in the default removes the only effect BYIELD ever had (the drain, #48). None of the planning docs state this; it also means the interleave tool's step 4 can never pass with the production split and should be rewritten rather than 'fixed'.
